### S$^2$Edit: Text-Guided Image Editing with Precise Semantic and Spatial Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04584v1)
- **Authors**: Xudong Liu, Zikun Chen, Ruowei Jiang, Ziyi Wu, Kejia Yin, Han Zhao, Parham Aarabi, Igor Gilitschenski
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have enabled high-quality generation and manipulation of images guided by texts, as well as concept learning from images. However, naive applications of existing methods to editing tasks that require fine-grained control, e.g., face editing, often lead to suboptimal solutions with identity information and high-frequency details lost during the editing process, or irrelevant image regions altered due to entangled concepts. In this work, we propose S$^2$Edit, a novel method based on a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model that enables personalized editing with precise semantic and spatial control. We first fine-tune our model to embed the identity information into a learnable text token. During fine-tuning, we disentangle the learned identity token from attributes to be edited by enforcing an orthogonality constraint in the textual feature space. To ensure that the identity token only affects regions of interest, we apply object masks to guide the cross-attention maps. At inference time, our method performs localized editing while faithfully preserving the original identity with semantically disentangled and spatially focused identity token learned. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of S$^2$Edit over state-of-the-art methods both quantitatively and qualitatively. Additionally, we showcase several compositional image editing applications of S$^2$Edit such as makeup transfer.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces S2Edit, a novel text-guided image editing method designed to provide precise semantic and spatial control while preserving the identity of the edited subject. S2Edit builds on pre-trained text-to-image diffusion models and consists of two stages: identity fine-tuning and inference. The key innovations lie in disentangling the learned identity token by enforcing an orthogonality constraint in the textual feature space and applying spatial control by masking the cross-attention maps during fine-tuning.  This allows for localized edits without losing identity or accidentally editing irrelevant image regions. The paper showcases S2Edit's superior performance over state-of-the-art methods both quantitatively and qualitatively, also demonstrating its application to compositional image editing tasks like makeup transfer.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several techniques to achieve a better balance between identity preservation and target prompt alignment. Individually, fine-tuning diffusion models for personalization, and using attention maps for spatial control are not entirely new concepts. However, the specific approach of combining a disentangled identity token learned through orthogonality constraints *with* masked cross-attention during fine-tuning to achieve fine-grained control in image editing is a significant contribution. The idea of explicitly disentangling the identity token from modifiable attributes is insightful. This addresses a key limitation in existing personalized editing methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in text-guided image editing: balancing identity preservation with accurate and faithful editing. Current methods often struggle with either altering the identity of the subject or failing to make the desired changes accurately. S2Edit's ability to overcome this challenge has significant implications for various applications, including personalized image editing, content creation, and even potential uses in areas like virtual try-on or avatar creation. The user study results, indicating a strong preference for S2Edit's output, further reinforces the practical relevance of this work. The compositional editing examples, like makeup transfer, show the potential for extending the method to more complex scenarios. The explicit attempt to balance identity preservation with target prompt alignment through the optimization objective shows a deliberate understanding of the problem domain.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods and motivates the need for a solution like S2Edit.
    *   **Technically Sound Approach:** The method is well-described, and the rationale behind each component (semantic and spatial control) is explained.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents comprehensive qualitative and quantitative comparisons demonstrating S2Edit's superiority. The user study provides additional validation.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The compositional editing examples showcase the potential of S2Edit for real-world applications.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies thoroughly analyze the contribution of each component to the overall performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Object Masks:** While the paper avoids requiring mask annotations by using the cross-attention of the object descriptor (e.g. "lady"), the method still depends on an object mask being reasonably obtainable. This could be a limitation in scenarios with complex or poorly defined objects.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Fine-tuning, although relatively fast at 95 seconds per image, adds an overhead compared to methods that operate purely at inference time.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Failure Cases:** While the results are generally strong, the paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of potential failure cases or limitations, beyond the limitations section. For example, cases where the object mask is inaccurate.

*   **Potential Influence:** S2Edit has the potential to influence future research in text-guided image editing by providing a more robust and controllable approach to personalized editing. The techniques of disentangled identity token learning and masked cross-attention could be adopted and extended in other contexts. The paper’s focus on preserving fine-grained details and its explicit effort to balance competing objective will be of interest to the community.

**Justification for Score:**

While not completely revolutionary, S2Edit represents a significant advancement in the field of text-guided image editing due to its practical effectiveness, well-reasoned approach, and comprehensive evaluation. The limitations are relatively minor, and the strengths outweigh them significantly. I believe that the paper is technically sound and that it represents a notable improvement upon the existing state of the art.

### any4: Learned 4-bit Numeric Representation for LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04610v1)
- **Authors**: Mostafa Elhoushi, Jeff Johnson
- **Abstract**: We present any4, a learned 4-bit weight quantization solution for large language models (LLMs) providing arbitrary numeric representations without requiring pre-processing of weights or activations. any4 yields higher accuracy compared to other related 4-bit numeric representation types: int4, fp4 and nf4, as evaluated on a range of model sizes, generations and families (Llama 2, Llama 3, Mistral and Mixtral). While any4 does not require preprocessing of weights or activations, it is also competitive with orthogonal techniques that require such preprocessing (e.g., AWQ and GPTQ). We also experiment with any3 and any2 and show competitiveness at lower bits. Additionally, we show that we can calibrate using a single curated diverse sample rather than hundreds of samples from a dataset as done in most quantization approaches. We also open source tinygemm, a latency optimized GPU matrix multiplication library for LLMs, that implements any4 using a GPU-efficient lookup table strategy along with other common quantization methods. We open source our code at https://github.com/facebookresearch/any4 .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "any4," a novel learned 4-bit weight quantization method for large language models (LLMs).  any4 stands out because it offers arbitrary numeric representations learned directly from the data, eliminating the need for manual weight or activation pre-processing often required by other quantization techniques. The method leverages a per-row codebook/lookup table (LUT) generated using a k-means-style alternating optimization to minimize the error in output activations, enhancing accuracy. Evaluations across various LLM families (Llama 2/3, Mistral/Mixtral) and sizes demonstrate that any4 achieves higher accuracy compared to standard 4-bit numeric formats like int4, fp4, and nf4, while also being competitive with more complex methods like AWQ and GPTQ. The paper also explores the use of any3 and any2 for lower bit quantization. Key contributions include a curated single-sample calibration method and the open-source release of tinygemm, a GPU matrix multiplication library optimized for LLMs with any4 support.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty primarily lies in the learned arbitrary numeric representation approach, specifically its implementation and application to LLMs without pre-processing requirements. While learned quantization isn't entirely new (references to Lloyd and Max in signal processing are made), its specific application to LLMs in this efficient manner and its hardware implementation (tinygemm) contribute significantly to the current state of the field. The use of a single, curated calibration sample, rather than relying on larger datasets like C4, is also a notable deviation from conventional approaches.
* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the efficiency of LLM inference.  The demonstrated accuracy gains over standard 4-bit quantization schemes and the competitiveness with more sophisticated methods (AWQ, GPTQ) are valuable. The open-sourcing of tinygemm provides a valuable resource for the community, enabling faster inference on GPUs using the proposed quantization technique. The reduction in reliance on pre-processing is an important practical advantage that can simplify deployment and reduce computational overhead.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper presents comprehensive results across multiple LLM families and sizes, consistently showing the advantages of any4.
    *   **Practical focus:** The method is designed for efficient hardware implementation, as evidenced by the tinygemm library.
    *   **Simplicity:** The elimination of pre-processing steps simplifies the quantization process.
    *   **Open-source contribution:**  The release of both the code and the optimized matrix multiplication library encourages further research and adoption.
    *   **Single Sample calibration:** The reduction in the required dataset allows for faster calibration
* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited exploration of combinations:** While the paper claims orthogonality with methods like AWQ and GPTQ, there's limited empirical investigation of combining any4 with these orthogonal approaches. The paper mentions that the existing schemes *can* theoretically be combined, but the performance impact of such combinations is left unexamined. This is important as many quantization works combine multiple techniques for optimal performance.
    *  **LUT Memory overhead:** The table indicates only tiny benefits over INT4 at the smaller sizes. The authors only evaluate large LUTs that add 0.06 bits/entry of memory overhead, making INT4's memory use almost as small as ANY4's while likely offering close levels of performance. Also, as the authors point out, the savings come from having to use an LUT anyway, but the LUT has an overhead. How is this best used?
    *   **Missing analysis of corner cases:**  It would be useful to understand the scenarios where other quantization methods might outperform any4. A deeper analysis of the characteristics of the weights and activations that make any4 successful (or less successful) would strengthen the paper.
    *  **No comparisons to mixed-precision quantization:** State of the art performance is often achieved by quantizing models to multiple precisions. How does any4 compare?

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research in LLM quantization by providing a competitive alternative to existing methods and highlighting the importance of learned numeric representations. The tinygemm library could become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners deploying LLMs. Further, the approach of using a single curated calibration sample could lead to more efficient and practical quantization workflows.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of LLM quantization by presenting a novel and practical approach. The empirical results are compelling, and the open-source release enhances the impact of the work. However, the limited investigation of combinations with orthogonal techniques, the lack of detailed analysis of corner cases, and the missing comparisons to mixed-precision quantization detract from its overall impact. The limited benefit relative to INT4 also detracts from the usefulness of ANY4, as INT4 is already heavily optimized. Therefore, a score of 7 is appropriate. While the paper has significant strengths, there is room for further investigation and refinement to fully realize its potential.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Information-Guided Diffusion Sampling for Dataset Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04619v1)
- **Authors**: Linfeng Ye, Shayan Mohajer Hamidi, Guang Li, Takahiro Ogawa, Miki Haseyama, Konstantinos N. Plataniotis
- **Abstract**: Dataset distillation aims to create a compact dataset that retains essential information while maintaining model performance. Diffusion models (DMs) have shown promise for this task but struggle in low images-per-class (IPC) settings, where generated samples lack diversity. In this paper, we address this issue from an information-theoretic perspective by identifying two key types of information that a distilled dataset must preserve: ($i$) prototype information $\mathrm{I}(X;Y)$, which captures label-relevant features; and ($ii$) contextual information $\mathrm{H}(X | Y)$, which preserves intra-class variability. Here, $(X,Y)$ represents the pair of random variables corresponding to the input data and its ground truth label, respectively. Observing that the required contextual information scales with IPC, we propose maximizing $\mathrm{I}(X;Y) + \beta \mathrm{H}(X | Y)$ during the DM sampling process, where $\beta$ is IPC-dependent. Since directly computing $\mathrm{I}(X;Y)$ and $\mathrm{H}(X | Y)$ is intractable, we develop variational estimations to tightly lower-bound these quantities via a data-driven approach. Our approach, information-guided diffusion sampling (IGDS), seamlessly integrates with diffusion models and improves dataset distillation across all IPC settings. Experiments on Tiny ImageNet and ImageNet subsets show that IGDS significantly outperforms existing methods, particularly in low-IPC regimes. The code will be released upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of dataset distillation, which aims to create a small, representative dataset that can train models to similar performance as the full dataset. The authors focus on improving diffusion model (DM)-based distillation, particularly in low images-per-class (IPC) settings, where DMs often struggle to generate diverse samples. They propose an information-theoretic approach, arguing that a distilled dataset should preserve both prototype information (label-relevant features) and contextual information (intra-class variability). They introduce Information-Guided Diffusion Sampling (IGDS), which maximizes a combination of mutual information (I(X;Y)) and conditional entropy (H(X|Y)) during the DM sampling process.  They develop a variational estimator (VE) using a neural network to estimate these information-theoretic quantities and guide the diffusion process. Experiments on Tiny ImageNet and ImageNet subsets demonstrate that IGDS outperforms existing methods, especially in low-IPC regimes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strengths:** The information-theoretic framing of the dataset distillation problem is a significant contribution. It provides a principled way to understand and address the diversity issues in low-IPC settings.  The idea of balancing prototype and contextual information based on IPC is clever.  The variational estimator approach is novel in the context of guiding diffusion models for dataset distillation.
*   **Weaknesses:** While the high-level information-theoretic ideas are solid, the practical implementation relies on neural network-based estimations (the variational estimator). The performance gains could be intertwined with the neural architectures of the diffusion model or the variational estimator, which reduces the generality of the theoretical claims. Furthermore, while the method improves performance in low-IPC settings, it builds upon existing diffusion models and dataset distillation techniques. The novelty is therefore incremental in improving a well-established pipeline.

**Significance:**

*   **Strengths:** Improving dataset distillation is crucial for reducing the computational and storage costs of training deep learning models. IGDS offers a promising approach for better performance in low-IPC regimes, which is a practical and relevant scenario. The method is shown to outperform existing methods.
*   **Weaknesses:** While the paper shows improvements on ImageNet and Tiny ImageNet, the practical impact of dataset distillation *in general* is still debated.  The improvements in lower IPC is good, but the overall performance for a variety of applications is not sufficiently shown. While model-agnostic in principle, the reliance on neural network based VEs means transfer performance to different models may be limited. The improved results are on image classification, which is a fairly specific application of DL. The practical significance depends on the generalizability of the proposed method across various data modalities (e.g., text, audio) and tasks (e.g., segmentation, object detection).

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing an information-theoretic approach to DM-based dataset distillation, particularly addressing the diversity issue in low-IPC regimes.  The novel variational estimator approach and the experimental results showcasing improved performance support the proposed method.  However, the novelty is largely incremental, relying heavily on existing diffusion models and dataset distillation techniques. The reliance on neural networks to estimate information-theoretic quantities presents potential challenges to generalizability and theoretical purity. Also, the practical significance and broad applicability of dataset distillation (even with IGDS) is still somewhat debatable. Therefore, a score between 6 and 7 is the most reasonable.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Multimodal LLM Integrated Semantic Communications for 6G Immersive Experiences
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04621v1)
- **Authors**: Yusong Zhang, Yuxuan Sun, Lei Guo, Wei Chen, Bo Ai, Deniz Gunduz
- **Abstract**: 6G networks promise revolutionary immersive communication experiences including augmented reality (AR), virtual reality (VR), and holographic communications. These applications demand high-dimensional multimodal data transmission and intelligent data processing in real-time, which is extremely challenging over resource-limited wireless communication systems. Moreover, a joint understanding of the environment, context, and user intent is essential to deliver task-relevant content effectively. This article presents a novel multimodal large language model (MLLM) integrated semantic communications framework, termed MLLM-SC, which fully leverages reasoning and generative capabilities of pre-trained foundation models for context-aware and task-oriented wireless communication. The MLLM-SC framework adopts a device-edge collaborative architecture. At the edge, MLLM-empowered semantic guidance module analyzes multimodal inputs, user intents, and channel conditions to generate importance-aware attention maps prioritizing semantically critical information. An importance-aware semantic encoder and a resource-adaptive semantic decoder are jointly designed and optimized, which can utilize the semantic guidance for adaptive bandwidth allocation and high-quality content reconstruction or generation. Extensive case studies on visual question answering for AR/VR applications and diffusion-driven image generation validate the effectiveness of MLLM-SC.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MLLM-SC, a novel framework integrating Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) into semantic communications for 6G immersive experiences. MLLM-SC utilizes a device-edge collaborative architecture where an MLLM at the edge analyzes multimodal inputs, user intents, and channel conditions to generate importance-aware attention maps. These maps guide an importance-aware semantic encoder and a resource-adaptive semantic decoder to prioritize semantically critical information for transmission and reconstruction/generation.  The framework is demonstrated with case studies in visual question answering for AR/VR applications and diffusion-driven image generation. Results suggest MLLM-SC effectively adapts bandwidth allocation and enhances content reconstruction quality compared to baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of integrating MLLMs with semantic communication is itself novel. While some prior works explored LLMs in semantic communication, this paper provides a comprehensive framework for *deeply* integrating MLLMs into the encoding, transmission, and decoding stages, using the MLLM for semantic *guidance*. The design of the importance-aware encoder/decoder, specifically tailored to exploit the MLLM's output, also appears novel. The use of dual-path encoders (high-fidelity and lightweight) and their dynamic adaptation is a potentially interesting contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial challenge in 6G: delivering high-quality immersive experiences (AR/VR/XR, holographic communications) over resource-constrained wireless links. Semantic communication, focusing on transmitting meaning rather than raw data, is a promising direction. By leveraging MLLMs, the system can potentially prioritize the most important information, drastically improving efficiency and user experience. The case studies, while preliminary, show a tangible benefit in VQA accuracy and image generation quality, suggesting practical relevance. However, there are significant scalability challenges to address.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Problem:** Clearly articulates the need for efficient multimodal data transmission in 6G.
    *   **Innovative Framework:** Proposes a coherent and potentially effective architecture for MLLM integration.
    *   **Practical Focus:** Demonstrates the framework's applicability through relevant case studies (AR/VR, diffusion models).
    *   **Good Experimental Evaluation:** Utilizes standard metrics (PSNR, SSIM, CLIP-score, IoU, LPIPS) to evaluate performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Experiments:** The case studies, while promising, are still relatively simple and require more extensive evaluation.  For instance, the VQA example "What is across the sea?" feels somewhat contrived and might not fully represent the complexity of real-world scenarios.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:**  MLLMs are computationally expensive.  The paper doesn't adequately address the computational overhead of running MLLMs at the edge and the impact on latency, a critical factor for immersive applications. Running these on edge servers may still not address battery life for end-user devices.
    *   **Lack of Quantitative Latency Analysis:** There is no performance quantification concerning the delay incurred in the complete encoding, MLLM analysis, and decoding chain. This must be explicitly addressed.
    *   **Implementation Details:** The paper describes the overall architecture, it lacks specific implementation details like the size of different layers and training methodologies, and model sizes.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the scalability issues can be addressed, MLLM-SC has the potential to significantly improve the performance of immersive communication systems in 6G and beyond. The framework could inspire further research on integrating large AI models into communication systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and promising architecture with potential for significant impact.  However, the limited scope of experiments, lack of comprehensive scalability analysis, absence of latency measurements, and lack of detailed model and training parameter configurations make it difficult to assess its true practical value. Therefore, I assign a score of **7**.

*   The core idea is good and relevant (4 points).
*   The experiments show initial promise, but need considerable expansion and analysis (2 points).
*   Scalability and implementation concerns need much greater attention (1 points deduction).

### Hierarchical Intent-guided Optimization with Pluggable LLM-Driven Semantics for Session-based Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04623v1)
- **Authors**: Jinpeng Chen, Jianxiang He, Huan Li, Senzhang Wang, Yuan Cao, Kaimin Wei, Zhenye Yang, Ye Ji
- **Abstract**: Session-based Recommendation (SBR) aims to predict the next item a user will likely engage with, using their interaction sequence within an anonymous session. Existing SBR models often focus only on single-session information, ignoring inter-session relationships and valuable cross-session insights. Some methods try to include inter-session data but struggle with noise and irrelevant information, reducing performance. Additionally, most models rely on item ID co-occurrence and overlook rich semantic details, limiting their ability to capture fine-grained item features. To address these challenges, we propose a novel hierarchical intent-guided optimization approach with pluggable LLM-driven semantic learning for session-based recommendations, called HIPHOP. First, we introduce a pluggable embedding module based on large language models (LLMs) to generate high-quality semantic representations, enhancing item embeddings. Second, HIPHOP utilizes graph neural networks (GNNs) to model item transition relationships and incorporates a dynamic multi-intent capturing module to address users' diverse interests within a session. Additionally, we design a hierarchical inter-session similarity learning module, guided by user intent, to capture global and local session relationships, effectively exploring users' long-term and short-term interests. To mitigate noise, an intent-guided denoising strategy is applied during inter-session learning. Finally, we enhance the model's discriminative capability by using contrastive learning to optimize session representations. Experiments on multiple datasets show that HIPHOP significantly outperforms existing methods, demonstrating its effectiveness in improving recommendation quality. Our code is available: https://github.com/hjx159/HIPHOP.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score and justification.

**Summary**

The paper "Hierarchical Intent-guided Optimization with Pluggable LLM-Driven Semantics for Session-based Recommendation" (HIPHOP) addresses limitations in existing session-based recommendation (SBR) models. It proposes a novel architecture incorporating a pluggable LLM-driven semantic embedding module to enrich item representations, a graph neural network (GNN) for modeling item transitions, a dynamic multi-intent capturing module to handle diverse user interests, and a hierarchical inter-session similarity learning module with intent-guided noise reduction.  Contrastive learning is used to further optimize session representations.  Experiments on public and newly created datasets demonstrate the superiority of HIPHOP over existing SBR methods.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty stems from the synergistic combination of several techniques rather than a completely groundbreaking individual component. While LLMs have been applied to recommendation before, the specific architecture of HIPHOP presents a fresh approach. The major novelties lie in the carefully constructed inter-session similarity learning module that combines both global and local session similarities along with a noise reduction mechanism guided by user intents. In addition, it constructs three novel SBR datasets with item semantic information. The "pluggable" design for LLM integration is also a positive element.

*   **Significance:** The improved performance of HIPHOP over strong baselines suggests its potential significance.  The hierarchical approach to inter-session similarity learning addresses the crucial problem of noisy data that is commonly an issue when using inter-session information. The new datasets created and released with the paper are also a valuable contribution to the field, allowing for future research to explore semantic enrichment in SBR.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive Architecture: HIPHOP provides a well-designed architecture that combines existing and new techniques into a coherent system.
    *   Semantic Enrichment: The use of LLMs to enhance item representations addresses a common limitation of SBR models that rely solely on item ID co-occurrence.
    *   Hierarchical Inter-Session Learning: The method for inter-session relationship learning appears to be the strongest element of this paper.
    *   Experimental Validation: The experiments are well-designed and include comparisons against a range of competitive baselines across multiple datasets. The newly created datasets help make this validation more valuable.
    *   Code and Data Availability: The open-source code and datasets increase reproducibility and allow other researchers to build upon this work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental Novelty: The paper builds upon existing techniques; it does not introduce a radically new paradigm for SBR.
    *   Complexity: The architecture is complex, which may make it difficult for practitioners to implement and deploy.
    *   LLM dependency: While LLMs have great capability for embedding generation, these embeddings can become unstable over time and require re-training.

*   **Justification for Score:**

While HIPHOP demonstrates impressive performance and introduces some valuable architectural components, its reliance on existing techniques and complexity holds it back from achieving a higher score. A crucial aspect of assessing novelty is the degree to which the work offers *fundamentally new* ways of thinking about the problem, and while HIPHOP is an advancement, it doesn't revolutionize the field. The gains are significant and practical, but they are based on a careful orchestration of previously-developed ideas with a novel noise-reduction component, not a break from prior approaches. The introduction of three new datasets is also significant, as it facilitates further research in the field by providing standardized benchmarks. Taking all of these elements into account, the paper represents a *strong and valuable contribution* to SBR, meriting a score that reflects both its advancements and limitations.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Knowledge-Aware Self-Correction in Language Models via Structured Memory Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04625v1)
- **Authors**: Swayamjit Saha
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are powerful yet prone to generating factual errors, commonly referred to as hallucinations. We present a lightweight, interpretable framework for knowledge-aware self-correction of LLM outputs using structured memory graphs based on RDF triples. Without retraining or fine-tuning, our method post-processes model outputs and corrects factual inconsistencies via external semantic memory. We demonstrate the approach using DistilGPT-2 and show promising results on simple factual prompts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Knowledge-Aware Self-Correction (KASC) framework to address the issue of factual inaccuracies (hallucinations) in Large Language Model (LLM) outputs.  The KASC framework uses a structured knowledge graph, encoded using RDF triples, as an external memory to verify and correct LLM outputs post-generation. The approach doesn't require retraining or fine-tuning the base LLM. The framework identifies factual inconsistencies by matching entity-relation pairs from the LLM output against known RDF triples, and revises the output accordingly. The authors demonstrate the framework using DistilGPT-2 and a small, hand-curated RDF graph, showing promising results on simple factual prompts. Key advantages highlighted are non-intrusiveness, interpretability, and extensibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using external knowledge to correct LLM outputs isn't entirely new, as Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) models already address this. The novelty lies in the **simplicity, modularity, and interpretability** of the proposed approach compared to RAG and knowledge editing methods. The exclusive use of RDF memory for symbolic, post-hoc correction, without treating the LLM as a retriever-agent hybrid, is a differentiating factor. Using symbolic reasoning is a way to perform fact-checking with more transparency, something that is very desirable and not fully solved yet in general. The approach's ability to retain the core LLM and apply external fixes seems very appropriate to many constrained and cost-effective scenarios.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem: reducing hallucinations in LLMs, which is crucial for trustworthy applications. The significance stems from the framework's lightweight and modular nature, making it particularly suitable for low-resource environments and applications requiring high transparency. The clear separation of fluency (handled by LLM) and factuality (handled by RDF graph) is a valuable contribution, making the system more understandable and auditable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and motivates the problem of LLM hallucinations.
    *   **Simple and Interpretable Approach:** The use of RDF triples provides a transparent and easily understandable way to represent knowledge and correct LLM outputs.
    *   **Modularity:** The post-processing nature of the correction layer allows it to be used with various LLMs and knowledge bases.
    *   **Positive Results:** The experiments show that the framework can successfully correct factual errors without significantly affecting the fluency of the generated text.
    *   **Focus on constrained environments:** It's particularly useful in low-resource or real-time applications, which emphasizes the importance of efficiency and transparency.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is conducted using DistilGPT-2 and a relatively small, hand-curated RDF graph. This limits the generalizability of the results to larger models and more complex knowledge domains. The prompt set is very limited, making it difficult to make broader conclusions.
    *   **Simple Entity Matching:** The reliance on strict string matching for entity resolution can lead to errors when dealing with aliases or paraphrases.
    *   **Lack of comparison to baselines:** The paper lacks a direct comparison with other hallucination mitigation techniques like lightweight RAG.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The framework's correction capabilities are limited to simple factual statements. It cannot address more complex reasoning errors or nuanced inaccuracies.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper offers a valuable perspective on how to improve the factual accuracy of LLMs in a modular and interpretable way. The KASC framework could serve as a foundation for future research on combining symbolic reasoning with LLMs and open the door for simple, verifiable techniques. The modularity principle opens an interesting pathway to make LLMs easier to trust.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the limited scope of the evaluation, the paper presents a well-motivated and clearly articulated approach to addressing a significant problem in LLMs. The simplicity, interpretability, and modularity of the KASC framework are valuable contributions. While the evaluation could be more comprehensive, the initial results are promising and the paper opens up interesting avenues for future research. Given these considerations, I'll assign a score of **6**.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Heterogeneous User Modeling for LLM-based Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04626v1)
- **Authors**: Honghui Bao, Wenjie Wang, Xinyu Lin, Fengbin Zhu, Teng Sun, Fuli Feng, Tat-Seng Chua
- **Abstract**: Leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) for recommendation has demonstrated notable success in various domains, showcasing their potential for open-domain recommendation. A key challenge to advancing open-domain recommendation lies in effectively modeling user preferences from users' heterogeneous behaviors across multiple domains. Existing approaches, including ID-based and semantic-based modeling, struggle with poor generalization, an inability to compress noisy interactions effectively, and the domain seesaw phenomenon. To address these challenges, we propose a Heterogeneous User Modeling (HUM) method, which incorporates a compression enhancer and a robustness enhancer for LLM-based recommendation. The compression enhancer uses a customized prompt to compress heterogeneous behaviors into a tailored token, while a masking mechanism enhances cross-domain knowledge extraction and understanding. The robustness enhancer introduces a domain importance score to mitigate the domain seesaw phenomenon by guiding domain optimization. Extensive experiments on heterogeneous datasets validate that HUM effectively models user heterogeneity by achieving both high efficacy and robustness, leading to superior performance in open-domain recommendation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of modeling user preferences from heterogeneous behaviors across multiple domains in open-domain recommendation, particularly when using Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors propose a Heterogeneous User Modeling (HUM) method that includes a compression enhancer and a robustness enhancer. The compression enhancer uses a tailored prompt and masking mechanism to compress heterogeneous behaviors into a tailored token and extract transferable knowledge. The robustness enhancer introduces a domain importance score to mitigate the domain seesaw phenomenon. The authors conduct experiments on heterogeneous datasets, demonstrating that HUM effectively models user heterogeneity, achieves high efficacy and robustness, and outperforms existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques used to address the challenges of heterogeneous user modeling with LLMs. While the individual components (prompt engineering, masking, domain weighting) have been explored in isolation, the specific architecture (compression enhancer, robustness enhancer) and the way these components are integrated demonstrate a novel approach. The explicit effort to address the "domain seesaw" effect is also a valuable contribution.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from the increasing importance of open-domain recommendation, where modeling diverse user behaviors is critical. By improving the way LLMs can handle heterogeneous data, the paper contributes to more accurate and robust recommendations in such environments. The detailed ablation study further contributes valuable insights into the importance of specific elements in an LLM-based recommendation system.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly identifies a relevant problem (heterogeneous user modeling in open-domain recommendation) and articulates the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   The proposed HUM method is well-defined and justified, with clear explanations of each component.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, including comparisons to strong baselines, ablation studies, and analyses of robustness, noise resistance, and generalization.
    *   The paper is well-written and organized.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a specific LLM architecture (Qwen) limits the generalizability of the findings. Exploring the efficacy of HUM with other LLMs, especially smaller or fine-tuned ones, would strengthen the paper.
    *   The domain importance score is relatively straightforward. More sophisticated approaches to domain weighting could be investigated.
    *   While the paper discusses cold-start, a more direct evaluation of cold-start performance would be beneficial.
    *   The novelty of the individual modules isn't groundbreaking, more novelty comes from how they are put together into a system.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future open-domain recommendation systems using LLMs. The HUM framework provides a blueprint for addressing the challenges of heterogeneous user modeling, and the insights gained from the experimental evaluation can guide further research. The approach is likely to be implemented in applications involving diversified user history in recommendation.
*   **Justification of Score:** While the components are not entirely novel individually, their thoughtful combination and engineering for heterogeneous user modeling in open-domain recommendation, as well as the clear and rigorous evaluation, justify a score above average. It's not revolutionary, but a solid contribution with immediate practical implications.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Prompt Difficulty be Online Predicted for Accelerating RL Finetuning of Reasoning Models?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04632v1)
- **Authors**: Yun Qu, Qi Cheems Wang, Yixiu Mao, Vincent Tao Hu, Xiangyang Ji
- **Abstract**: Recent advances have witnessed the effectiveness of reinforcement learning (RL) finetuning in enhancing the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). The optimization process often requires numerous iterations to achieve satisfactory performance, resulting in high computational costs due to the need for frequent prompt evaluations under intensive LLM interactions and repeated policy updates. Appropriate online prompt selection methods reduce iteration steps by prioritizing informative prompts during training, while the pipeline's reliance on exhaustive prompt evaluation and subset selection for optimization still incurs substantial computational overhead due to frequent LLM inference calls. Distinguished from these direct evaluate-then-select schemes, this work investigates iterative approximate evaluation for arbitrary prompts and introduces Model Predictive Prompt Selection (MoPPS), a Bayesian risk-predictive framework that online estimates prompt difficulty without requiring costly LLM interactions. Technically, MoPPS models each prompt's success rate as a latent variable, performs streaming Bayesian inference, and employs posterior sampling in a constructed multi-armed bandit machine, enabling sample efficient and adaptive prompt selection. Extensive experiments across mathematics, planning, and vision-based geometry tasks show that MoPPS reliably predicts prompt difficulty and accelerates training with significantly reduced LLM rollouts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Model Predictive Prompt Selection (MoPPS), a novel framework for accelerating reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs). MoPPS addresses the computational expense of existing online prompt selection methods, which require frequent LLM inferences to evaluate prompt difficulty. Instead, MoPPS utilizes a Bayesian risk-predictive framework to online estimate prompt difficulty without LLM interactions. It models each prompt's success rate as a latent variable, performs streaming Bayesian inference, and employs posterior sampling in a constructed multi-armed bandit machine for sample-efficient and adaptive prompt selection.  Extensive experiments across various reasoning tasks (mathematics, planning, geometry) demonstrate that MoPPS effectively predicts prompt difficulty and accelerates training with significantly reduced LLM rollouts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in amortizing prompt evaluation for RL finetuning of LLMs.  Existing online prompt selection methods generally evaluate prompt difficulty via direct LLM interactions, which is computationally expensive. MoPPS's use of a Bayesian surrogate model to predict prompt difficulty, combined with a multi-armed bandit approach for efficient exploration, offers a computationally efficient alternative. The idea of using MPTS for RL finetuning is interesting. Applying a beta distribution and Thompson sampling isn't entirely novel, but the specific combination and adaptation to the prompt selection problem for LLMs makes this work innovative.

* **Significance:** The problem addressed is significant. RL finetuning of LLMs is computationally intensive. Reducing LLM rollouts directly translates to reduced costs and faster development cycles.  The experimental results show substantial performance improvements and computational savings (e.g., up to 1.8x speedup on Countdown, 26% improvement on AIME24, and comparable performance to dynamic sampling with drastically fewer rollouts). The impact on the field could be substantial if MoPPS proves to be generalizable and applicable across diverse LLM architectures and reasoning tasks.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Computational Efficiency:** MoPPS demonstrably reduces LLM inference calls, a major bottleneck in RL finetuning.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments are thorough, covering multiple reasoning tasks and LLM sizes. The performance improvements and computational savings are clearly presented.
    * **Principled Approach:** The use of Bayesian inference and multi-armed bandits provides a solid theoretical foundation for the framework.
    * **Algorithm Agnostic**: The experiments demonstrate that the MoPPS framework is compatible with different RL finetuning algorithms.
    * **Adaptability:** By leveraging online posterior updates, the framework is able to adapt to non-stationary training dynamics.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Simplifying Assumptions:** The reliance on binary reward signals is a simplification. While the authors state that it can be extended to richer reward forms, the practical challenges of doing so are not fully addressed.  How well would the algorithm work with fine-grained reward structures?
    * **Dependence on Hyperparameters:** The algorithm has hyperparameters like the decay factor λ, the target success rate γ*, and the number of candidate batches *B*. Tuning these hyperparameters requires domain knowledge or significant experimentation. While the authors provide a sensitivity analysis, the optimal settings may vary substantially across different tasks and datasets.
    * **Limited Exploration of Alternative Bandit Strategies**: While Thompson sampling is a reasonable choice, it isn't necessarily optimal for all scenarios. Other bandit algorithms (e.g., UCB) might offer superior exploration-exploitation trade-offs in specific settings.
    * **Limited analysis of scalability:** While impressive gains are demonstrated on specific task, the paper does not explore if the approach scales well as the model complexity increase. It is unclear what would the benefits of this technique be for larger and more complex models.

* **Potential Influence:** MoPPS has the potential to become a widely adopted technique for accelerating RL finetuning of LLMs. The reduction in computational costs makes RL finetuning more accessible. The modular design of MoPPS allows it to be easily integrated with existing RL algorithms and LLM architectures. The paper also provides a solid foundation for future research on amortized prompt evaluation and adaptive sampling strategies in LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper presents a genuinely novel and impactful approach to address a significant bottleneck in RL finetuning of LLMs. The experimental results are convincing, the theoretical foundation is sound, and the potential influence on the field is considerable. However, simplifying assumptions, sensitivity to hyperparameters, and limited scaling analysis prevent the paper from receiving a higher score. The lack of an examination of edge cases and the limitations of the chosen methodology prevent the assignment of a higher score. Although significant, there are important assumptions that can reduce the adoption rate and must be carefully considered when evaluating results.

### MODA: MOdular Duplex Attention for Multimodal Perception, Cognition, and Emotion Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04635v1)
- **Authors**: Zhicheng Zhang, Wuyou Xia, Chenxi Zhao, Zhou Yan, Xiaoqiang Liu, Yongjie Zhu, Wenyu Qin, Pengfei Wan, Di Zhang, Jufeng Yang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) recently showed strong capacity in integrating data among multiple modalities, empowered by a generalizable attention architecture. Advanced methods predominantly focus on language-centric tuning while less exploring multimodal tokens mixed through attention, posing challenges in high-level tasks that require fine-grained cognition and emotion understanding. In this work, we identify the attention deficit disorder problem in multimodal learning, caused by inconsistent cross-modal attention and layer-by-layer decayed attention activation. To address this, we propose a novel attention mechanism, termed MOdular Duplex Attention (MODA), simultaneously conducting the inner-modal refinement and inter-modal interaction. MODA employs a correct-after-align strategy to effectively decouple modality alignment from cross-layer token mixing. In the alignment phase, tokens are mapped to duplex modality spaces based on the basis vectors, enabling the interaction between visual and language modality. Further, the correctness of attention scores is ensured through adaptive masked attention, which enhances the model's flexibility by allowing customizable masking patterns for different modalities. Extensive experiments on 21 benchmark datasets verify the effectiveness of MODA in perception, cognition, and emotion tasks. Source code and demo are available in https://zzcheng.top/MODA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MODA: MOdular Duplex Attention for Multimodal Perception, Cognition, and Emotion Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of "attention deficit disorder" in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), where inconsistent cross-modal attention and layer-by-layer decay of attention activation hinder performance on tasks requiring fine-grained cognition and emotion understanding. To combat this, the authors propose a novel attention mechanism called Modular Duplex Attention (MODA). MODA simultaneously conducts inner-modal refinement and inter-modal interaction, using a "correct-after-align" strategy. The key components are the Duplex V/T-Aligner, which maps tokens into duplex modality spaces, and a Modular Masked Attention component, which allows for customizable masking patterns.  The paper presents extensive experiments on 21 benchmark datasets demonstrating MODA's effectiveness in perception, cognition, and emotion tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the proposed MODA architecture, specifically the duplex attention alignment and modular masked attention mechanisms. The "correct-after-align" strategy and the explicit separation of self-modal and cross-modal interactions are interesting design choices. The idea of aligning tokens based on gram matrix space bases is also a notable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant and significant challenge in the field of MLLMs: improving performance on tasks that demand a deeper understanding of modalities and their interplay, going beyond simple classification. If MODA proves to be generally applicable and scalable, it could contribute to progress in applications like nuanced dialogue systems, emotion recognition in human-computer interaction, and advanced image understanding.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the "attention deficit disorder" problem, providing a compelling motivation for the proposed solution.
    *   **Technical Approach:**  The MODA architecture is well-defined, with clear explanations of its components and their functionalities. The duplex alignment and masked attention techniques are innovative.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The evaluation is comprehensive, covering a wide range of datasets and tasks.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different MODA components.
    *   **Comparison to State-of-the-Art:** The paper compares MODA against strong baseline models, showing performance gains on several benchmarks.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The visualization examples are helpful for understanding how MODA addresses the limitations of existing MLLMs, showing both cognitive conversation analysis and emotion understanding.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** MODA introduces added complexity to the attention mechanism. While the paper claims linear complexity for some components, a more detailed analysis of computational overhead is needed.
    *   **Dependency on CLIP:** The model relies on CLIP for visual encoding, and the performance may be directly tied to the capabilities of CLIP. It's not clear whether MODA provides any advantages beyond the CLIP encoder.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the authors provide intuition and empirical validation, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of why MODA works, especially regarding the duplex alignment, could strengthen the paper.
    *   **Reproducibility:**  Although the authors claim to provide source code, the accessibility and usability of the code are critical for reproducibility and wider adoption.
    *   **Comparison against Finetuned baselines:** The evaluation mainly considers generic pre-trained models. It would be beneficial to compare MODA against the best finetuned counterparts.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of MLLMs. The problem addressed is important, the proposed solution is technically sound, and the experimental results are promising. However, the increased complexity, dependency on CLIP, and lack of deeper theoretical justification limit the potential impact. It's worth noting that the empirical evaluation demonstrates that this module is valuable in improving certain vision and emotion recognition understanding based on the numbers presented in the ablation study. The significance should be weighed in combination to the novelty which justifies our final score.

### VectorLLM: Human-like Extraction of Structured Building Contours vis Multimodal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04664v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Zhang, Shiqing Wei, Shihao Chen, Wenling Yu, Muying Luo, Shunping Ji
- **Abstract**: Automatically extracting vectorized building contours from remote sensing imagery is crucial for urban planning, population estimation, and disaster assessment. Current state-of-the-art methods rely on complex multi-stage pipelines involving pixel segmentation, vectorization, and polygon refinement, which limits their scalability and real-world applicability. Inspired by the remarkable reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs), we introduce VectorLLM, the first Multi-modal Large Language Model (MLLM) designed for regular building contour extraction from remote sensing images. Unlike existing approaches, VectorLLM performs corner-point by corner-point regression of building contours directly, mimicking human annotators' labeling process. Our architecture consists of a vision foundation backbone, an MLP connector, and an LLM, enhanced with learnable position embeddings to improve spatial understanding capability. Through comprehensive exploration of training strategies including pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, and preference optimization across WHU, WHU-Mix, and CrowdAI datasets, VectorLLM significantly outperformed the previous SOTA methods by 5.6 AP, 7.1 AP, 13.6 AP, respectively in the three datasets. Remarkably, VectorLLM exhibits strong zero-shot performance on unseen objects including aircraft, water bodies, and oil tanks, highlighting its potential for unified modeling of diverse remote sensing object contour extraction tasks. Overall, this work establishes a new paradigm for vector extraction in remote sensing, leveraging the topological reasoning capabilities of LLMs to achieve both high accuracy and exceptional generalization. All the codes and weights will be published for promoting community development.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VectorLLM, a novel multimodal large language model (MLLM) designed for extracting structured building contours directly from remote sensing imagery. Unlike existing methods that rely on complex multi-stage pipelines, VectorLLM regresses building contours corner-point by corner-point, mimicking human annotators. The architecture consists of a vision backbone, an MLP connector, and an LLM enhanced with learnable positional embeddings.  The paper explores various training strategies like pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, and preference optimization.  The results demonstrate significant performance improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods on WHU, WHU-Mix, and CrowdAI datasets. Furthermore, VectorLLM exhibits strong zero-shot performance on unseen object types.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Introduction of LLMs to Vector Extraction:** The most significant novelty lies in introducing LLMs to the field of vector extraction in remote sensing. This is a departure from traditional deep learning approaches.
*   **End-to-End Approach:** VectorLLM simplifies the process by directly regressing corner points rather than using intermediate representations like segmentation masks.  This end-to-end approach is a welcome simplification.
*   **Use of Learnable Positional Embeddings:** The use of learnable positional embeddings within the MLLM framework to improve spatial understanding is a notable architectural choice.

**Significance:**

*   **Performance Improvement:** The reported performance gains compared to existing state-of-the-art methods (P2PFormer, Line2Poly) are substantial, showcasing the potential of the LLM-based approach.
*   **Generalization and Zero-Shot Capabilities:** The strong zero-shot performance on unseen objects (aircraft, water bodies) is a significant finding, highlighting the potential for creating more generalized and adaptable models. This is a critical advantage over specialized approaches tailored to single tasks.
*   **New Paradigm for Vector Extraction:** VectorLLM represents a new paradigm for vector extraction in remote sensing by leveraging topological reasoning capabilities of LLMs, which could open avenues for future research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing methods and motivates the need for a novel approach.
*   **Well-defined methodology:** The architecture and training strategies are explained in detail, enabling reproducibility and further research.
*   **Comprehensive experimental evaluation:** Thorough experimentation across multiple datasets and ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of VectorLLM.
*   **Strong results:** Significant performance improvements on multiple benchmarks, coupled with impressive zero-shot generalization, are compelling.
*   **Potential for future research:** The work opens new avenues for exploration in LLM-based vector extraction and object understanding from remote sensing.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Bounding Box Dependence:** The reliance on bounding box detectors is a limitation, as detector errors can propagate to the contour extraction stage. A truly end-to-end system that eliminates this dependency would be even more desirable. While the paper mentions testing performance with a detector, this aspect should be emphasized as a practical limitation.
*   **Failure Cases:** The paper acknowledges failure cases (large buildings, complex structures), but further investigation into these failures and potential mitigation strategies would be beneficial. The limitation on handling buildings requiring multiple polygons is noteworthy.
*   **Computational cost:** While the paper focuses on performance and accuracy, discussion on the computational cost and resource requirements of training and inference with VectorLLM is lacking. This is a crucial factor for real-world deployment, especially for large LLMs.
*   **Dataset Limitations:** The annotations from CrowdAI are known to be inconsistent. Testing on more robust/accurate building footprint datasets, if available, would further strengthen the validity of findings.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of remote sensing object extraction.  The demonstrated success of LLMs could motivate other researchers to explore similar approaches for various geospatial analysis tasks. However, the reliance on pre-trained LLMs (which themselves are continuously evolving) also means that future work may be heavily influenced by the improvements (and limitations) of these foundational models.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution, it is not without its limitations. The dependence on a bounding box detector, failure cases with certain types of buildings, and a lack of information on computational costs limit its immediate practical applicability. However, the approach’s success on relatively simple structures offers a stepping stone for further refinement. The core idea of utilizing an LLM for vector extraction is ingenious and has substantial performance impacts. This, coupled with its robust experimentation, and strong zero-shot performance, justify a high score.
