### Hybrid Adversarial Spectral Loss Conditional Generative Adversarial Networks for Signal Data Augmentation in Ultra-precision Machining Surface Roughness Prediction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04665v1)
- **Authors**: Suiyan Shang, Chi Fai Cheung, Pai Zheng
- **Abstract**: Accurate surface roughness prediction in ultra-precision machining (UPM) is critical for real-time quality control, but small datasets hinder model performance. We propose HAS-CGAN, a Hybrid Adversarial Spectral Loss CGAN, for effective UPM data augmentation. Among five CGAN variants tested, HAS-CGAN excels in 1D force signal generation, particularly for high-frequency signals, achieving >0.85 wavelet coherence through Fourier-domain optimization. By combining generated signals with machining parameters, prediction accuracy significantly improves. Experiments with traditional ML (SVR, RF, LSTM) and deep learning models (BPNN, 1DCNN, CNN-Transformer) demonstrate that augmenting training data with 520+ synthetic samples reduces prediction error from 31.4% (original 52 samples) to ~9%, effectively addressing data scarcity in UPM roughness prediction."
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary, a critical evaluation of the novelty and significance, a score, and a justification for that score for the given paper.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited training data in ultra-precision machining (UPM) surface roughness prediction. It proposes a novel Hybrid Adversarial Spectral Loss Convolutional Conditional Generative Adversarial Network (HAS-CGAN) framework for data augmentation.  The HAS-CGAN enhances signal fidelity, particularly in higher frequencies, through Fourier-domain error penalization. The generated signals are then used, along with machining parameters, as input to various machine learning models for surface roughness prediction.  The results show that augmenting the training data with HAS-CGAN generated samples significantly improves prediction accuracy compared to using only the original limited dataset. The CNN-Transformer model, trained with the augmented data, achieves the best prediction performance. The key innovation is the introduction of spectral loss to the CGAN framework, which improves the quality of generated data by focusing on frequency-domain fidelity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Contribution:** The paper introduces a spectral loss function within a CGAN framework (HAS-CGAN) to address the limitations of traditional CGANs in generating high-fidelity signals for UPM surface roughness prediction. This addition is a significant improvement because surface roughness is highly sensitive to frequency components.
    *   **Originality:** While CGANs have been used in other domains for data augmentation, their application to UPM surface roughness prediction, especially with the incorporation of spectral loss, appears to be relatively novel. The specific combination of spectral loss and adversarial training for enhancing signal fidelity in this particular application seems to be original.
    *   **Incremental vs. Breakthrough:** The work is more of an incremental improvement than a breakthrough. It builds upon existing CGAN architectures and adapts them to a specific problem domain by adding a customized loss function.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Impact:** The research addresses a crucial problem in UPM: the scarcity of labeled data for training accurate prediction models. UPM data is costly to collect. By demonstrating the effectiveness of HAS-CGAN for data augmentation, the paper offers a practical solution for improving prediction accuracy even with limited data. This is particularly significant given the increasing importance of real-time quality control in advanced manufacturing.
    *   **Generality:** While the paper focuses on UPM surface roughness prediction, the HAS-CGAN framework with spectral loss could potentially be applied to other domains where signal data with frequency-dependent characteristics are used for prediction or classification tasks.
    *   **Practicality:** The approach is potentially practical, as it leverages existing deep learning techniques and adds a customized loss function. The implementation should be relatively straightforward for researchers or engineers familiar with CGANs and signal processing.
    *   **Weaknesses:**
        *   Lack of Theoretical Analysis: The paper could benefit from more in-depth theoretical analysis of why spectral loss improves signal fidelity in this specific context. A deeper mathematical explanation of the interaction between adversarial training and frequency-domain constraints would strengthen the results.
        *   Limited comparison scope: Compared to basic CGANs architectures, the research scope of physics-aware methods is somehow limitied.
        *   Limited Dataset: While the dataset is limited, the paper does not have some methods to enhance this problem such as using physics-aware domain knowledge as constraint.
        *   Limited application cases: The application has some limitation for wider range of UPM cases.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by presenting a practical solution to the data scarcity problem in UPM surface roughness prediction. The HAS-CGAN framework, with its novel integration of spectral loss, demonstrably improves the quality of generated signals and enhances prediction accuracy. The experiments are well-conducted, and the results are clearly presented.

However, the work is more of an incremental improvement than a groundbreaking innovation. While the addition of spectral loss is significant, the overall architecture is based on existing CGAN frameworks. The paper could be strengthened by a more in-depth theoretical analysis of the method, a more comprehensive exploration of hyperparameter tuning, and a broader evaluation across diverse UPM scenarios. The study is also slightly constrained by the original dataset's limited size, though the proposed solution effectively addresses the limitation. Furthermore, the generalizability of the framework to other signal processing tasks should be further explored. The significance of UPM is not as significant as semiconductor area.

In summary, the paper presents a valuable and practical solution to a relevant problem, warranting a score of 7. The strengths lie in its novelty of approach and clear demonstration of effectiveness, while the weaknesses relate to the depth of analysis, generalizability, and the limitations inherent in the initial dataset.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### ChangeBridge: Spatiotemporal Image Generation with Multimodal Controls for Remote Sensing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04678v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenghui Zhao, Chen Wu, Di Wang, Hongruixuan Chen, Zhuo Zheng
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in generative methods, especially diffusion models, have made great progress in remote sensing image synthesis. Despite these advancements, existing methods have not explored the simulation of future scenarios based on given scenario images. This simulation capability has wide applications for urban planning, land managementChangeBridge: Spatiotemporal Image Generation with Multimodal Controls, and beyond. In this work, we propose ChangeBridge, a conditional spatiotemporal diffusion model. Given pre-event images and conditioned on multimodal spatial controls (e.g., text prompts, instance layouts, and semantic maps), ChangeBridge can synthesize post-event images. The core idea behind ChangeBridge is to modeling the noise-to-image diffusion model, as a pre-to-post diffusion bridge. Conditioned on multimodal controls, ChangeBridge leverages a stochastic Brownian-bridge diffusion, directly modeling the spatiotemporal evolution between pre-event and post-event states. To the best of our knowledge, ChangeBridge is the first spatiotemporal generative model with multimodal controls for remote sensing. Experimental results demonstrate that ChangeBridge can simulate high-fidelity future scenarios aligned with given conditions, including event and event-driven background variations. Code will be available.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper, and here's my summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ChangeBridge, a novel spatiotemporal image generation model for remote sensing. Unlike existing methods that primarily focus on noise-to-image synthesis or modify existing conditions, ChangeBridge aims to simulate future scenarios by generating post-event images conditioned on pre-event imagery and multimodal spatial controls (text prompts, instance layouts, semantic maps).  The core innovation lies in a "conditional spatiotemporal diffusion bridge," which models the noise-to-image diffusion process as a pre-to-post event transition.  This allows direct modeling of spatiotemporal evolution under multiple constraints. Experiments demonstrate ChangeBridge's ability to generate high-fidelity future scenarios that are well-aligned with the provided conditions, improving performance in downstream tasks like change detection.  The authors emphasize that ChangeBridge is the first spatiotemporal generative model with multimodal controls for remote sensing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The claim that ChangeBridge is the "first spatiotemporal generative model with multimodal controls for remote sensing" is significant *if* true.  While many generative remote sensing papers exist, the unique combination of *spatiotemporal evolution modeling from pre- to post-event* coupled with *multimodal control* seems to be the key differentiating factor. The architecture directly models this pre-to-post transition, which is a novel contribution over existing techniques.  The "diffusion bridge" concept applied to this context also adds to novelty. The use of multiple conditions within one unified framework represents a valuable advance. It's important to verify the uniqueness of this combination by comparing it to more papers in the change detection/generation field, but based on the information at hand, it seems like it is a valuable and original contribution.

* **Significance:** The ability to simulate future scenarios has clear value in applications like urban planning, land management, and scenario forecasting.  The experimental results showing improved change detection performance are compelling, demonstrating the practical utility of ChangeBridge as a data augmentation technique. The gains are significant, achieving a good increase in IoU on relevant datasets. The downstream task improvement provides more evidence that generated imagery is relevant to the real world, not just visually compelling.  The approach could potentially allow for simulating the impact of various policy decisions or environmental factors on land cover, aiding in more informed decision-making.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined architecture with a novel diffusion bridge approach.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets and control modalities.
    *   Demonstrated improvement in downstream change detection tasks.
    *   Addresses a significant gap in the current remote sensing generation landscape.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper assumes a level of familiarity with diffusion models and related concepts, which might make it less accessible to a broader audience.
    *   The method's computational cost and scalability aren't thoroughly discussed. Diffusion models can be computationally intensive, and this aspect should be addressed.
    *   The experiments primarily focus on visual quality and change detection accuracy. Additional metrics related to the realism and diversity of the generated scenarios would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   The qualitative results, although visually appealing, could benefit from more in-depth analysis of the types of changes being generated and their alignment with the control signals.

* **Potential Influence:** ChangeBridge has the potential to become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners in remote sensing, urban planning, and land management. It could also spur further research into spatiotemporal generative models and their applications in various fields. It provides a strong baseline and sets a clear direction for future research.
* **Scope** The paper lacks in scope with the baseline. The number of relevant and recent publications it uses as a starting point is limited.
* **Baselines** The authors use few and somewhat outdated baselines.

**Justification for Score:**

The combination of novelty and significance warrants a fairly high score. The unique spatiotemporal diffusion bridge and the demonstrable improvements in change detection suggest that ChangeBridge is a valuable contribution. However, the limitations regarding scope, computational cost, and more in-depth scenario analysis, and baselines prevent it from being a truly exceptional work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TeethGenerator: A two-stage framework for paired pre- and post-orthodontic 3D dental data generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04685v1)
- **Authors**: Changsong Lei, Yaqian Liang, Shaofeng Wang, Jiajia Dai, Yong-Jin Liu
- **Abstract**: Digital orthodontics represents a prominent and critical application of computer vision technology in the medical field. So far, the labor-intensive process of collecting clinical data, particularly in acquiring paired 3D orthodontic teeth models, constitutes a crucial bottleneck for developing tooth arrangement neural networks. Although numerous general 3D shape generation methods have been proposed, most of them focus on single-object generation and are insufficient for generating anatomically structured teeth models, each comprising 24-32 segmented teeth. In this paper, we propose TeethGenerator, a novel two-stage framework designed to synthesize paired 3D teeth models pre- and post-orthodontic, aiming to facilitate the training of downstream tooth arrangement networks. Specifically, our approach consists of two key modules: (1) a teeth shape generation module that leverages a diffusion model to learn the distribution of morphological characteristics of teeth, enabling the generation of diverse post-orthodontic teeth models; and (2) a teeth style generation module that synthesizes corresponding pre-orthodontic teeth models by incorporating desired styles as conditional inputs. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that our synthetic dataset aligns closely with the distribution of real orthodontic data, and promotes tooth alignment performance significantly when combined with real data for training. The code and dataset are available at https://github.com/lcshhh/teeth_generator.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TeethGenerator, a novel two-stage framework for generating paired pre- and post-orthodontic 3D dental models. This addresses the significant bottleneck of data scarcity in training AI models for digital orthodontics. The first stage utilizes a VQ-VAE and diffusion model to generate diverse, anatomically plausible post-orthodontic (aligned) teeth models. The second stage uses a Transformer to generate corresponding pre-orthodontic (misaligned) models based on a provided "style" (misalignment pattern), while preserving the shape and size of individual teeth. Experiments demonstrate that the synthetic data improves the performance of downstream tooth alignment tasks when used in conjunction with real data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to generating paired orthodontic data. While individual components (diffusion models, VQ-VAEs, Transformers) are not new, their specific combination and adaptation for this task, coupled with the unique constraints of generating multi-instance, anatomically consistent teeth models, demonstrate novelty. The explicit handling of tooth consistency (matching shapes between pre- and post-treatment models) and style diversity are also positive aspects. The idea of predicting transformation parameters to "misalign" post-treatment teeth models based on a style model (instead of generating pre-orthodontic data from scratch) is a clever approach.
*   **Significance:** The limited availability of paired pre/post orthodontic 3D data is a well-recognized problem. Overcoming this data scarcity would be highly beneficial for advancing research in automated tooth alignment and treatment planning. The paper shows a tangible benefit of using the synthetic data to improve the performance of a tooth alignment network (TANet), which further solidifies the significance of the work. If the synthetic data can also be shown to improve the training of orthodontists and technicians (as the authors suggest), this would add more weight to the significance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The two-stage approach is well-reasoned and addresses specific challenges in generating realistic and consistent teeth models.
    *   The experiments demonstrate a clear improvement in downstream task performance when using the synthetic data.
    *   The ablation studies offer insights into the design choices and the importance of various components.
    *   The paper provides a good qualitative evaluation of the generated models.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the code is promised to be publicly available, at the time of the arXiv submission, it was not. Open-sourcing the code and data would greatly improve reproducibility and impact.
    *   The comparisons are limited to general 3D shape generation methods. A comparison with existing tooth alignment methods by generating synthetic data and training them on it (if possible) would further demonstrate the value of the paper.
    *   While the metrics used (1-NNA and uniqueness) are standard, it would be beneficial to have orthodontists evaluate the generated data for clinical plausibility. This would strengthen the argument about the usefulness of the synthetic dataset.
    *   The method still relies on some real data for training the VQ-VAE and Transformer. It would be valuable to explore the performance of the system with limited or no real data, pushing the boundaries of synthetic data generation further.
*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to accelerate research in digital orthodontics by providing a means to generate large-scale datasets. It could enable the development of more robust and accurate tooth alignment algorithms and assist in training and education. If the generated data proves to be clinically relevant and useful to orthodontists, the impact could be substantial.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by addressing a critical data scarcity problem in digital orthodontics. The proposed TeethGenerator framework is novel and demonstrably improves the performance of downstream tooth alignment tasks. However, the absence of a publicly available code and data, limitations in the evaluation metrics (lack of clinical validation), and limited comparison to tooth alignment methods prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AKEGEN: A LLM-based Tabular Corpus Generator for Evaluating Dataset Discovery in Data Lakes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04687v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenwei Dai, Chuan Lei, Asterios Katsifodimos, Xiao Qin, Christos Faloutsos, Huzefa Rangwala
- **Abstract**: How to generate a large, realistic set of tables along with joinability relationships, to stress-test dataset discovery methods? Dataset discovery methods aim to automatically identify related data assets in a data lake. The development and evaluation of such solutions for customers from a wide range of business domains, relies on diverse, high quality and domain-specific tabular benchmarks. Large language models (LLMs) are trained on a wide variety of text data, which can provide a strong foundation of general and domain-specific knowledge. In this paper, we ask the question -- \textit{can we leverage LLMs to generate a tabular benchmark adequate for evaluating the dataset discovery solutions?} In particular, we focus on the task of finding joinable tables which is the cornerstone of virtually every dataset discovery method. Current corpora for evaluating dataset discovery methods are mainly based on subsets of open data, and they suffer from three important issues: $i)$ they focus on very common and generic data types (e.g., address, id, name, etc.); $ii)$ they do not contain human-annotated column pairs; instead, practitioners synthesize ground truth using table splits (e.g., horizontal for table union search and vertical ones for joinability) and $iii)$ they do not focus on semantic column relationships.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LAKEGEN, a novel LLM-based tabular corpus generator specifically designed for evaluating dataset discovery methods in data lakes.  LAKEGEN utilizes domain-specific ontologies to define table schemas and relationships, leverages LLMs to populate the tables with realistic data, and employs semantic perturbations to introduce complexity and noise.  The authors highlight the shortcomings of existing tabular datasets for dataset discovery, which often lack domain specificity, human-annotated column relationships, and focus on simple data types. LAKEGEN generates datasets for finance, pharma, and healthcare domains and demonstrates that these datasets pose challenges to existing dataset discovery methods, suggesting that current benchmarks are insufficient for truly evaluating the effectiveness of these methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel elements. The combination of ontologies for schema generation, LLMs for data population, and semantic perturbations for introducing noise is a unique approach to creating tabular benchmarks. LAKEGEN's focus on generating domain-specific datasets addresses a crucial gap in the existing landscape. However, each individual component is not entirely new.  The use of LLMs for data generation has been explored in other contexts, and table perturbation techniques are known. The originality lies in the combined application and the specific focus on creating benchmarks for *dataset discovery*.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate to high. The lack of realistic and diverse benchmarks is a major obstacle to the development and evaluation of effective dataset discovery methods. LAKEGEN has the potential to address this problem by providing a platform for generating high-quality, domain-specific benchmarks that can better reflect the challenges encountered in real-world data lakes. By demonstrating the inadequacies of existing benchmarks, the paper also raises awareness and motivates the need for more sophisticated evaluation strategies. The ability to train and fine-tune dataset discovery models on synthetic, yet realistic data, is valuable especially in domains where real data access is restricted due to privacy or regulatory concerns.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a real problem:** The paper identifies a critical gap in the existing ecosystem of dataset discovery methods – the need for realistic and domain-specific benchmarks.
    *   **Novel approach:** The combination of ontologies, LLMs, and semantic perturbations provides a compelling approach to generating tabular data.
    *   **Practical evaluation:** The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of LAKEGEN by showing that it can generate datasets that pose challenges to existing dataset discovery methods.
    *   **Clear explanation and structure:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the motivation, approach, and results.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on LLMs:** The quality of the generated datasets depends heavily on the capabilities of the underlying LLM. If the LLM is poorly trained or lacks sufficient domain knowledge, the resulting data may be unrealistic.
    *   **Manual Ontology Curation:** Scaling LAKEGEN to many more diverse domains would likely require significant manual effort in curating and adapting relevant ontologies.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of LLM Generated Values:** Although the paper uses a prompt to generate values, it does not conduct a rigorous evaluation to measure its efficacy. It could have investigated various prompts and LLM techniques and performed an error analysis to examine the quality of its table values.
    *   **Complexity:** The framework involves multiple components (ontology, LLM prompting, perturbation), which adds to its implementation complexity.

*   **Potential Influence:** LAKEGEN has the potential to influence the field by providing a much-needed resource for evaluating dataset discovery methods. The framework can be used by researchers to develop and compare new methods, and by practitioners to evaluate the performance of existing methods on their own data. It can also motivate further research into the use of LLMs for synthetic data generation and data augmentation in data discovery tasks.

*   **Score Justification:** Given the novelty of the combined approach, the significance of the problem addressed, and the potential for influence, but also acknowledging the LLM dependence and limitations of evaluation of LLM value generation, a score of **7** seems appropriate. While the paper does not present a fundamentally groundbreaking idea, it makes a significant contribution by providing a valuable resource and highlighting the need for more realistic benchmarks in dataset discovery.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### Structure-Guided Diffusion Models for High-Fidelity Portrait Shadow Removal
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04692v1)
- **Authors**: Wanchang Yu, Qing Zhang, Rongjia Zheng, Wei-Shi Zheng
- **Abstract**: We present a diffusion-based portrait shadow removal approach that can robustly produce high-fidelity results. Unlike previous methods, we cast shadow removal as diffusion-based inpainting. To this end, we first train a shadow-independent structure extraction network on a real-world portrait dataset with various synthetic lighting conditions, which allows to generate a shadow-independent structure map including facial details while excluding the unwanted shadow boundaries. The structure map is then used as condition to train a structure-guided inpainting diffusion model for removing shadows in a generative manner. Finally, to restore the fine-scale details (e.g., eyelashes, moles and spots) that may not be captured by the structure map, we take the gradients inside the shadow regions as guidance and train a detail restoration diffusion model to refine the shadow removal result. Extensive experiments on the benchmark datasets show that our method clearly outperforms existing methods, and is effective to avoid previously common issues such as facial identity tampering, shadow residual, color distortion, structure blurring, and loss of details. Our code is available at https://github.com/wanchang-yu/Structure-Guided-Diffusion-for-Portrait-Shadow-Removal.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Structure-Guided Diffusion Models for High-Fidelity Portrait Shadow Removal":

**Summary:**

This paper presents a diffusion-based approach for removing cast shadows from portrait images. The core idea is to treat shadow removal as an inpainting problem, leveraging the generative power of diffusion models. The approach consists of three main components:

1.  **Shadow-Independent Structure Extraction Network (SE-Net):** Trained on synthetic paired data (well-lit and relighted portraits) to extract a structure map that captures facial details while excluding shadow boundaries. The synthetic data is generated using a relighting-based data synthesis strategy.

2.  **Structure-Guided Inpainting Diffusion Model:** This diffusion model is conditioned on the structure map extracted by the SE-Net and is trained to reconstruct randomly masked shadow-free portraits.

3.  **Gradient-Guided Detail Restoration Diffusion Model:**  To restore fine-scale details (e.g., eyelashes, moles) potentially lost during the inpainting process, a separate diffusion model is trained to refine the shadow removal output, guided by the gradients within the original shadow regions.

The authors demonstrate through experiments on benchmark datasets that their method outperforms existing shadow removal techniques, addressing common issues like identity tampering, shadow residuals, color distortion, structure blurring, and detail loss.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strength:** The paper's main novelty lies in its clever combination of several existing ideas into a portrait-specific shadow removal pipeline.  Casting shadow removal as inpainting with diffusion models is not entirely new, but the *specific adaptation* to portraits using a structure-guided approach, followed by a detail restoration stage, is a significant contribution.  The relighting-based data synthesis strategy is a worthwhile method for constructing suitable training data.
*   **Weakness:**  Each individual component of the pipeline (structure extraction, diffusion-based inpainting, detail restoration) relies on established techniques. The architecture used for each model is based on previous methods. The authors leverage existing diffusion models with U-Net architecture.

**Significance:**

*   **Strength:** The paper addresses a practically relevant problem: improving the visual quality of portrait photos taken in uncontrolled environments.  Portrait shadow removal has real-world applications in photography, image editing, and potentially in improving the performance of face-related computer vision tasks. The ability to produce high-fidelity results while preserving identity and detail is particularly significant. The method appears to be robust to varying lighting conditions and inaccurate masks.
*   **Weakness:**  The quantitative results, while demonstrating improvement over other methods, don't necessarily translate into dramatically better *perceptual* quality in all cases. Visual inspection reveals instances where the improvements are subtle. While the paper addresses common issues like shadow residual and color distortion, it's not clear if it completely solves these problems in all scenarios. The reported failure case indicates limitations with handling complex self-attached shadows. The method may not be readily extensible to real-time processing due to the computational cost of diffusion models. Furthermore, the code provided is not fully accessible, as the real-world paired portrait datasets are restricted for sharing due to privacy concerns.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.

*   **Reasons for a positive score (7):**
    *   The paper provides a coherent and well-engineered pipeline for a challenging problem with demonstrated improvements in quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   The structure-guided approach is a sound and effective way to adapt diffusion-based inpainting to the specific requirements of portrait shadow removal.
    *   The data synthesis strategy is appropriate for the task.
    *   The paper demonstrates a clear understanding of the limitations of existing methods and proposes a solution to address them.
    *   The approach is applicable in practice.
*   **Reasons for not assigning a higher score (above 7):**
    *   The individual components are based on well-established techniques.  The contribution is more in the *combination* of ideas and careful design of the pipeline than in revolutionary innovations in any one component.
    *   The failure cases highlight limitations in handling complex shadowing scenarios.
    *   While the quantitative improvements are measurable, the visual differences aren't always dramatic and there is still room for refinement, particularly in perceptual quality.

In conclusion, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field of portrait image processing. While not groundbreaking in its individual components, the overall system is thoughtfully designed, well-executed, and delivers tangible improvements over existing methods. Its potential impact lies in its practical applicability and the improved visual quality it offers for portrait shadow removal.
