In this work, we propose a cognitively inspired perspective that reinterprets hallucinations as trajectory drift within a latent alignment space. Empirical observations reveal that generation unfolds within a multiaxial cognitive tension field, where the model must continuously negotiate competing demands across three key critical axes: semantic coherence, structural alignment, and knowledge grounding. We then formalize this three-axis space as the \textbf{Hallucination Tri-Space} and introduce the Alignment Risk Code (ARC): a dynamic vector representation that quantifies real-time alignment tension during generation. The magnitude of ARC captures overall misalignment, its direction identifies the dominant failure axis, and its imbalance reflects tension asymmetry. Based on this formulation, we develop the TensionModulator (TM-ARC): a lightweight controller that operates entirely in latent space. TM-ARC monitors ARC signals and applies targeted, axis-specific interventions during the sampling process. Extensive experiments on standard T2I benchmarks demonstrate that our approach significantly reduces hallucination without compromising image quality or diversity. This framework offers a unified and interpretable approach for understanding and mitigating generative failures in diffusion-based T2I systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Taming the Tri-Space Tension: ARC-Guided Hallucination Modeling and Control for Text-to-Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the persistent problem of hallucinations in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. It argues that these hallucinations are not random artifacts but rather reflect structured misalignments within the generative process. The core idea is to reinterpret hallucinations as trajectory drift within a latent alignment space, which they formalize as the "Hallucination Tri-Space." This space is spanned by three axes: Semantic Coherence, Structural Alignment, and Knowledge Grounding. They introduce the "Alignment Risk Code" (ARC), a dynamic vector representation that quantifies real-time alignment tension during generation. Based on ARC, they develop "TensionModulator (TM-ARC)," a lightweight controller that operates in latent space, monitors ARC signals, and applies axis-specific interventions during sampling. The paper presents extensive experiments demonstrating that TM-ARC reduces hallucinations without sacrificing image quality or diversity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the **conceptual framing of hallucinations as a dynamic process of tension and drift** within a structured tri-space. This is a significant departure from previous works that often treat hallucinations as isolated failures or post-hoc correction problems. The ARC vector provides a novel way to *quantify* this tension during the generative process and opens avenues to control and mitigate the issue.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in its potential to provide a **principled and interpretable approach** to understanding and mitigating generative failures in T2I systems. This goes beyond just fixing errors after they occur. Instead, the framework offers the possibility to *proactively manage* the generative process, making it more reliable and controllable. This makes the results more aligned with the prompt. Also, the plug-and-play nature of the TM-ARC controller means that models don't have to be retrained in order to be used with the controller.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong conceptual framework:** The Hallucination Tri-Space and ARC provide a powerful and intuitive framework for analyzing and understanding hallucinations.
    *   **Quantitative measure:** The ARC allows for quantitative monitoring of the alignment tension during generation.
    *   **Effective controller:** The TM-ARC demonstrates significant improvements in reducing hallucinations without compromising image quality or diversity, as demonstrated by experiments.
    *   **Generalizability:** TM-ARC’s ability to generalize across different architectures, datasets, and prompts through the modular nature, further underscores its significance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:**  While elegant, the framework has a certain level of complexity, which may not be easily understood by all researchers.
    *   **Limited quantitative metrics for qualitative improvements:** It's hard to quantify visual enhancements.
    *   **Dependency on ARC:** The effectiveness of TM-ARC is entirely dependent on the ARC, so an accurate representation of ARC is key.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in T2I generation by:
    *   Shifting the focus from post-hoc fixes to proactive alignment management.
    *   Encouraging the development of more interpretable and controllable generative models.
    *   Providing a new framework for analyzing and mitigating generative failures.

* **Justification for score**:
The conceptual formulation of the Hallucination Tri-Space and ARC, along with the successful implementation of the TM-ARC controller, represent a notable advancement in the field of T2I generation. While there are limitations, the proposed framework has a potential to pave way for interpretable, controllable and safe generative systems. The shift in perspective, from merely correcting hallucinations to actively managing alignment during the process, is promising. Therefore, I assign the paper a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DC-AR: Efficient Masked Autoregressive Image Generation with Deep Compression Hybrid Tokenizer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04947v1)
- **Authors**: Yecheng Wu, Junyu Chen, Zhuoyang Zhang, Enze Xie, Jincheng Yu, Junsong Chen, Jinyi Hu, Yao Lu, Song Han, Han Cai
- **Abstract**: We introduce DC-AR, a novel masked autoregressive (AR) text-to-image generation framework that delivers superior image generation quality with exceptional computational efficiency. Due to the tokenizers' limitations, prior masked AR models have lagged behind diffusion models in terms of quality or efficiency. We overcome this limitation by introducing DC-HT - a deep compression hybrid tokenizer for AR models that achieves a 32x spatial compression ratio while maintaining high reconstruction fidelity and cross-resolution generalization ability. Building upon DC-HT, we extend MaskGIT and create a new hybrid masked autoregressive image generation framework that first produces the structural elements through discrete tokens and then applies refinements via residual tokens. DC-AR achieves state-of-the-art results with a gFID of 5.49 on MJHQ-30K and an overall score of 0.69 on GenEval, while offering 1.5-7.9x higher throughput and 2.0-3.5x lower latency compared to prior leading diffusion and autoregressive models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DC-AR, a novel masked autoregressive (AR) text-to-image generation framework designed for high computational efficiency and superior image quality. DC-AR addresses limitations in previous AR models by proposing DC-HT, a deep compression hybrid tokenizer that achieves a high 32x spatial compression ratio while maintaining good reconstruction fidelity and cross-resolution generalization. Building on DC-HT, DC-AR creates a hybrid image generation process that first generates structural elements using discrete tokens and then refines the image with residual tokens. Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art results on benchmarks such as MJHQ-30K and GenEval, along with 1.5-7.9x higher throughput and 2.0-3.5x lower latency compared to previous state-of-the-art models. The core contribution is an end-to-end framework that integrates a compression tokenizer and a hybrid generation process using discrete and residual tokens, providing an efficient image generation pipeline.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the combination of existing techniques into a unified and highly optimized framework. The deep compression hybrid tokenizer (DC-HT) isn't entirely new (builds upon DC-AE and HART), but the specific three-stage adaptation training strategy to train the hybrid tokenizer and achieve good reconstruction quality is a solid contribution. The two-stage autoregressive generation framework itself borrows from existing MaskGIT implementations, but the careful integration with the deep compression tokenizer to generate a coarse and fine representation is a notable advance. While individual components may not be groundbreaking on their own, the clever system-level design to obtain an efficient text-to-image pipeline has significance.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key challenge in autoregressive image generation: balancing image quality with computational efficiency. The impressive throughput and latency improvements demonstrated by DC-AR are significant. High compression tokenization in autoregressive models has been a bottleneck. The results suggest DC-AR can bridge the quality gap with diffusion models while exceeding them in terms of speed, which is particularly important for real-time applications. The experiments are thorough and demonstrate the effectiveness of DC-AR against strong baselines. The detailed ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong experimental results:** The reported metrics on standard benchmarks are compelling and clearly demonstrate the benefits of DC-AR.
    *   **Thorough ablation studies:** These help to validate design choices and highlight the importance of each component of the framework.
    *   **Well-written and clearly presented:** The paper is easy to understand and follows a logical structure.
    *   **Addresses an important problem:**  Efficiency is a crucial aspect of text-to-image generation, and the paper offers a practical solution to improve it.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental novelty:** While the combination of components is clever, the individual components are built upon existing work, limiting the fundamental theoretical contribution.
    *   **Dependency on specific architectural choices:** DC-AR's performance might be highly dependent on the chosen tokenizer and transformer architecture. More analysis of architectural sensitivity might increase generalizability.
    *   **Scalability Analysis**:  It could be strengthened by analysis that shows performance increase with scaling parameters.

*   **Potential Impact:** The ideas presented in DC-AR could inspire new approaches to efficient autoregressive image generation, potentially leading to real-time applications and more accessible text-to-image tools. The DC-HT tokenizer and the hybrid generation approach could be adopted and extended in other contexts. The insights regarding the number of steps required for discrete and continuous tokens could inform future research directions.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**  The paper delivers a significant engineering contribution through a well-designed system that achieves state-of-the-art performance. The careful combination of existing components, along with the key contribution of the 3 stage training, has yielded a very efficient text-to-image generation model. While the paper's individual components aren't revolutionary, the system-level novelty and substantial performance gains justify a score of 7.5. The detailed ablation studies help improve reproducibility and trustworthiness. Future research will benefit from investigating better scalability and architectural analysis.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ArtifactsBench: Bridging the Visual-Interactive Gap in LLM Code Generation Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04952v1)
- **Authors**: Chenchen Zhang, Yuhang Li, Can Xu, Jiaheng Liu, Ao Liu, Shihui Hu, Dengpeng Wu, Guanhua Huang, Kejiao Li, Qi Yi, Ruibin Xiong, Haotian Zhu, Yuanxing Zhang, Yuhao Jiang, Yue Zhang, Zenan Xu, Bohui Zhai, Guoxiang He, Hebin Li, Jie Zhao, Le Zhang, Lingyun Tan, Pengyu Guo, Xianshu Pang, Yang Ruan, Zhifeng Zhang, Zhonghu Wang, Ziyan Xu, Zuopu Yin, Wiggin Zhou, Chayse Zhou, Fengzong Lian
- **Abstract**: The generative capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) are rapidly expanding from static code to dynamic, interactive visual artifacts. This progress is bottlenecked by a critical evaluation gap: established benchmarks focus on algorithmic correctness and are blind to the visual fidelity and interactive integrity that define modern user experiences. To bridge this gap, we introduce ArtifactsBench, a new benchmark and paradigm for the automated, multimodal evaluation of visual code generation. Our framework programmatically renders each generated artifact and captures its dynamic behavior through temporal screenshots. This visual evidence, alongside the source code, is then assessed by a Multimodal LLM (MLLM)-as-Judge, which is rigorously guided by a fine-grained, per-task checklist to ensure holistic and reproducible scoring. We construct a new benchmark of 1,825 diverse tasks and evaluate over 30 leading LLMs. Our automated evaluation achieves a striking 94.4% ranking consistency with WebDev Arena, the gold-standard for human preference in web development, and over 90% pairwise agreement with human experts. This establishes ArtifactsBench as the first framework to reliably automate the assessment of human-perceived quality at scale. Our analysis provides a high-resolution map of the current SOTA, revealing that generalist models often outperform domain-specific ones. We open-source ArtifactsBench, including the benchmark, evaluation harness, and baseline results at https://artifactsbenchmark.github.io/, to provide the community with a scalable and accurate tool to accelerate the development of user-centric generative models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ArtifactsBench: Bridging the Visual-Interactive Gap in LLM Code Generation Evaluation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ArtifactsBench, a new benchmark and evaluation framework specifically designed to assess the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in generating interactive visual artifacts, such as web widgets, data visualizations, and mini-games.  The authors argue that existing code generation benchmarks primarily focus on algorithmic correctness and functional task completion, neglecting the crucial aspects of visual fidelity, interactive integrity, and dynamic behavior inherent in modern user interfaces.  ArtifactsBench addresses this gap by rendering generated code, capturing dynamic behavior through screenshots, and then using a Multimodal LLM (MLLM)-as-Judge, guided by a fine-grained checklist, to evaluate both the code and the visual output.  The benchmark consists of 1,825 diverse tasks across nine domains, stratified by complexity.  The authors demonstrate that their automated evaluation achieves high ranking consistency with human preferences, and they provide an extensive analysis of over 30 leading LLMs, revealing insights such as the superior performance of generalist models over domain-specific ones in this context. The benchmark, evaluation harness, and baseline results are open-sourced.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strength:** The most significant novelty lies in the holistic approach to evaluating code generation. Moving beyond traditional metrics of functional correctness or static analysis of visual designs, ArtifactsBench introduces a dynamic and interactive assessment. The creation of an MLLM-as-Judge, guided by checklists and operating on rendered output, is a fresh approach in this area.

*   **Weakness:**  While the concept of using LLMs for evaluation is not entirely new, ArtifactsBench's specific implementation with screenshots, dynamic rendering, and the type of artifacts being evaluated, represents a good improvement.

**Significance:**

*   **Strength:** The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck in the development of LLMs for visual and interactive applications.  By providing a scalable and reliable evaluation framework, it enables targeted improvements in model architecture, training methodologies, and task-specific prompting. The ability to automatically assess human-perceived quality at scale is a major benefit to the field. Furthermore, the open-sourcing of the benchmark promotes accessibility and collaboration within the research community. The discovery that generalist models outperform specialized ones offers valuable insights for model development strategies.

*   **Weakness:** The evaluation relies heavily on the performance of the MLLM-as-Judge. There is a risk that the MLLM-as-Judge may introduce its own biases or have limitations in its understanding of aesthetic design principles, which may not always perfectly align with human preferences. Though the paper addresses this by demonstrating agreement with human ratings, there is still a dependency on the capabilities of a potentially evolving technology. The types of artifacts might lean towards standard UI elements rather than more complex, creative visual designs. The "checklist-driven" aspect, while adding consistency, might limit the MLLM's ability to value completely novel, but appropriate, outputs.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-designed benchmark that tackles a relevant and growing problem in the LLM space. The methodology is clearly articulated, and the experimental results are compelling, demonstrating high consistency with human preferences. The open-source nature of the project further amplifies its potential impact.  However, the inherent reliance on the performance and potential biases of the MLLM-as-Judge is a point of concern. The benchmark can be further improved to include a more diverse and challenging set of visual tasks and to explore alternative evaluation metrics that go beyond checklist-based assessments.

**Score: 8**

The ArtifactsBench paper makes a significant contribution by providing a scalable and human-aligned evaluation framework for LLM-generated visual artifacts, marking a significant step forward in the field. Its innovative methodology and detailed analysis offer valuable insights and accelerate progress toward more capable and user-centric generative models. The few weaknesses mentioned above can be areas for further improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LAPS-Diff: A Diffusion-Based Framework for Singing Voice Synthesis With Language Aware Prosody-Style Guided Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04966v1)
- **Authors**: Sandipan Dhar, Mayank Gupta, Preeti Rao
- **Abstract**: The field of Singing Voice Synthesis (SVS) has seen significant advancements in recent years due to the rapid progress of diffusion-based approaches. However, capturing vocal style, genre-specific pitch inflections, and language-dependent characteristics remains challenging, particularly in low-resource scenarios. To address this, we propose LAPS-Diff, a diffusion model integrated with language-aware embeddings and a vocal-style guided learning mechanism, specifically designed for Bollywood Hindi singing style. We curate a Hindi SVS dataset and leverage pre-trained language models to extract word and phone-level embeddings for an enriched lyrics representation. Additionally, we incorporated a style encoder and a pitch extraction model to compute style and pitch losses, capturing features essential to the naturalness and expressiveness of the synthesized singing, particularly in terms of vocal style and pitch variations. Furthermore, we utilize MERT and IndicWav2Vec models to extract musical and contextual embeddings, serving as conditional priors to refine the acoustic feature generation process further. Based on objective and subjective evaluations, we demonstrate that LAPS-Diff significantly improves the quality of the generated samples compared to the considered state-of-the-art (SOTA) model for our constrained dataset that is typical of the low resource scenario.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LAPS-Diff paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LAPS-Diff, a diffusion-based Singing Voice Synthesis (SVS) framework specifically designed for the low-resource scenario of Bollywood Hindi singing. The key contributions involve: (1) Curating a new Hindi SVS dataset. (2) Leveraging pre-trained language models (IndicBERT and XPhoneBERT) to enhance lyrical content representation via word and phone-level embeddings. (3) Incorporating style and pitch losses using a style encoder and a pre-trained pitch extraction model. (4) Utilizing MERT and IndicWav2Vec embeddings as conditional priors for the denoiser to improve acoustic feature generation. The authors demonstrate improvements over the DiffSinger baseline through objective and subjective evaluations, showcasing the effectiveness of their approach in capturing linguistic content, vocal style, and pitch variations in a low-resource setting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the paper builds upon the established DiffSinger framework, it introduces several novel components tailored for the specific challenges of Bollywood Hindi SVS. The use of IndicBERT and XPhoneBERT to capture language-aware features is a valuable addition. The application of MERT and IndicWav2Vec as conditional priors within the diffusion process is also a noteworthy contribution. Using CCC is a standard method and not considered novel. While many of the individual components aren't groundbreaking, their integration within a diffusion model for a specific, low-resource language makes the system novel.

*   **Significance:** The most significant aspect of the paper is its focus on a low-resource language (Hindi) and a specific musical genre (Bollywood). Most SVS research concentrates on high-resource languages like Mandarin, Japanese, Korean and English, so addressing this gap is important.  The proposed LAPS-Diff model shows promising results, indicating its potential for generating more natural and expressive singing voices in resource-constrained scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Targeted approach:** The framework is well-suited to the challenges of Bollywood Hindi SVS.
    *   **Clear description:** The paper clearly explains the architecture and training process.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The objective and subjective evaluations provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. The ablation study helps to demonstrate the importance of each component.
    *   **Dataset creation:** Curating and processing a new Hindi SVS dataset is a valuable contribution to the research community.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset Size:** Though not explicitly negative, 1 hour is still considered small. As the community moves toward more zero-shot approaches the resource constraints are less concerning.
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** Relying on pre-trained language and acoustic models can limit the model's adaptability to other languages or genres where such models are not readily available. However, these pretrained models are critical to the system's overall success.
    *   **Limited Generality Evaluation:** While the system is tuned for Bollywood Hindi songs, the paper does not evaluate its generalization performance to other genres or languages.
    *   **Incremental improvements on baseline:** While the results show an improvement, the individual improvements are not substantial. They're a combination of the models working in tandem.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a positive impact on the SVS research community, particularly for researchers working on low-resource languages or specific musical genres. The proposed framework can serve as a template for adapting diffusion-based SVS models to other resource-constrained settings.

*   **Justification for Score:**

Given the novel integration of existing components within the DiffSinger framework, the focus on a low-resource language and genre, and the promising experimental results, a score of 7 is warranted. The enhancements are not drastically transformative, and there are minor limitations. Nonetheless, the work makes a valuable contribution to the field by providing a practical and effective approach for SVS in resource-constrained scenarios.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Case for Instance-Optimized LLMs in OLAP Databases
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04967v1)
- **Authors**: Bardia Mohammadi, Laurent Bindschaedler
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) can enhance analytics systems with powerful data summarization, cleaning, and semantic transformation capabilities. However, deploying LLMs at scale -- processing millions to billions of rows -- remains prohibitively expensive in computation and memory. We present IOLM-DB, a novel system that makes LLM-enhanced database queries practical through query-specific model optimization. Instead of using general-purpose LLMs, IOLM-DB generates lightweight, specialized models tailored to each query's specific needs using representative data samples. IOLM-DB reduces model footprints by up to 76% and increases throughput by up to 3.31$\times$ while maintaining accuracy through aggressive compression techniques, including quantization, sparsification, and structural pruning. We further show how our approach enables higher parallelism on existing hardware and seamlessly supports caching and batching strategies to reduce overheads. Our prototype demonstrates that leveraging LLM queries inside analytics systems is feasible at scale, opening new possibilities for future OLAP applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces IOLM-DB, a system designed to efficiently integrate Large Language Models (LLMs) into OLAP databases.  The core idea is to create instance-optimized LLMs tailored to specific queries and datasets, rather than relying on general-purpose LLMs.  IOLM-DB achieves this through a combination of model compression techniques like quantization, sparsification, and pruning, which reduces memory footprint and increases throughput while aiming to maintain accuracy.  A prototype is built using Python's pandas library and evaluated on three representative workloads: text summarization, data correction, and fuzzy joins. The results demonstrate significant model size reduction and throughput improvements, indicating the potential of the approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of instance-optimizing LLMs specifically for database queries is a valuable contribution. While model compression techniques like quantization and pruning are known, their application and adaptation to the *specific context* of OLAP database queries, with workload and data distribution-aware optimization, provides a novel angle. Combining these known techniques and applying them specifically to LLM integration within database queries warrants the claimed novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge: the high computational cost of integrating LLMs into databases at scale. By making LLM-enhanced database queries more practical, IOLM-DB has the potential to open up new possibilities for OLAP applications, such as semantic data transformations, smarter data cleaning, and more sophisticated summarization. The ability to run LLM-powered operations efficiently directly within the database engine has a significant advantage over exporting data for external processing. This has the potential to transform how data is managed and analyzed in OLAP environments.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The problem addressed is well-motivated and relevant to current trends in data management and AI.
    *   The approach is clearly explained and technically sound.
    *   The evaluation, while preliminary, provides evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed techniques. The gains in throughput are significant.
    *   The paper identifies clear avenues for future work, like optimizing the vLLM cache and improving the integration with pandas.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation is limited. The three use cases are somewhat narrow in scope, and the datasets, while real-world, could be more diverse. It would be useful to benchmark using a wider range of query complexities and data scales.
    *   The baseline is limited to the full Llama-3.1-Instruct-8B model. Comparing against other LLM optimization techniques, even if not tailored to databases, would strengthen the results.
    *   The paper doesn't delve deeply into the trade-offs between accuracy, model size, and throughput. More detailed analysis of these trade-offs would be valuable.
    *   While the general approach is solid, the current implementation is only a prototype. It remains to be seen how well it scales to a production database environment.
    * The method relies on predictability of OLAP workloads. While claimed as a strength it could be limiting in a real world scenario.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could spur further research into instance-optimized LLMs for database applications. The techniques described could be adapted and improved upon by other researchers. Also, it will encourage the community to explore specialized optimization strategies and accelerate the development of new analytics and transformation techniques for diverse workloads.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant approach to integrating LLMs into OLAP databases. The initial results are promising, demonstrating the potential for significant performance improvements. The weaknesses are primarily related to the limited scope of the evaluation and the early stage of the prototype. Despite these limitations, the core idea is strong and has the potential to have a real impact on the field. The paper provides a solid foundation for future research and development in this area. Therefore, a score of 7 is warranted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Video LLMs Refuse to Answer? Alignment for Answerability in Video Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04976v1)
- **Authors**: Eunseop Yoon, Hee Suk Yoon, Mark A. Hasegawa-Johnson, Chang D. Yoo
- **Abstract**: In the broader context of deep learning, Multimodal Large Language Models have achieved significant breakthroughs by leveraging powerful Large Language Models as a backbone to align different modalities into the language space. A prime exemplification is the development of Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). While numerous advancements have been proposed to enhance the video understanding capabilities of these models, they are predominantly trained on questions generated directly from video content. However, in real-world scenarios, users often pose questions that extend beyond the informational scope of the video, highlighting the need for Video-LLMs to assess the relevance of the question. We demonstrate that even the best-performing Video-LLMs fail to reject unfit questions-not necessarily due to a lack of video understanding, but because they have not been trained to identify and refuse such questions. To address this limitation, we propose alignment for answerability, a framework that equips Video-LLMs with the ability to evaluate the relevance of a question based on the input video and appropriately decline to answer when the question exceeds the scope of the video, as well as an evaluation framework with a comprehensive set of metrics designed to measure model behavior before and after alignment. Furthermore, we present a pipeline for creating a dataset specifically tailored for alignment for answerability, leveraging existing video-description paired datasets.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Can Video LLMs Refuse to Answer? Alignment for Answerability in Video Large Language Models" addresses a significant gap in the capabilities of Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). While these models excel at processing video content and generating responses based on it, they fall short in recognizing and rejecting irrelevant or unanswerable questions posed by users. The authors argue that this limitation stems from the lack of training targeting question relevance assessment. To mitigate this issue, the paper introduces a novel framework called "alignment for answerability," which allows Video-LLMs to evaluate the relevance of user questions in relation to the input video and decline to answer when appropriate. Furthermore, the authors propose a new evaluation framework, complete with metrics to assess model performance before and after implementing this alignment. They also describe a method for creating a dataset specifically designed for training Video-LLMs to improve their answerability capabilities. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper provides a meaningful contribution by highlighting an important oversight in the current training and application of Video-LLMs. The novel proposition of alignment for answerability is compelling because it addresses real-world scenarios where users might ask questions that go beyond the video's scope, which has not been previously explored in-depth for multimodal models.  **Strengths:** 1. **Relevance:** The challenge of irrelevant question handling is pertinent for user experiences and model reliability. 2. **Novelty:** Introducing a framework specifically focused on evaluating the validity of questions marks a significant step forward in model alignment. 3. **Evaluation Metrics:** The development of a set of metrics to assess improvements offers a clear pathway for measuring the success of the proposed approach. 4. **Dataset Generation:** The creation of a dedicated dataset adds practical value and facilitates future research. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Generalizability:** The framework’s effectiveness in diverse real-world applications outside of the tested scenarios is not sufficiently explored. 2. **Training Cost:** The implications of training this new alignment on computational efficiency and resource demands are not addressed. 3. **Broader Impact:** There could be a discussion on potential ethical concerns regarding the models refusing to answer and the transparency of such refusals. In conclusion, while the paper presents a well-structured proposal to enhance Video-LLM capabilities significantly, it suffers slightly from a lack of depth in exploring broader implications and practical outcomes. The potential for influencing future research in multimodal AI and real-world applications is evident, but challenges regarding implementation and generalization remain.  **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### TLB-VFI: Temporal-Aware Latent Brownian Bridge Diffusion for Video Frame Interpolation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04984v1)
- **Authors**: Zonglin Lyu, Chen Chen
- **Abstract**: Video Frame Interpolation (VFI) aims to predict the intermediate frame $I_n$ (we use n to denote time in videos to avoid notation overload with the timestep $t$ in diffusion models) based on two consecutive neighboring frames $I_0$ and $I_1$. Recent approaches apply diffusion models (both image-based and video-based) in this task and achieve strong performance. However, image-based diffusion models are unable to extract temporal information and are relatively inefficient compared to non-diffusion methods. Video-based diffusion models can extract temporal information, but they are too large in terms of training scale, model size, and inference time. To mitigate the above issues, we propose Temporal-Aware Latent Brownian Bridge Diffusion for Video Frame Interpolation (TLB-VFI), an efficient video-based diffusion model. By extracting rich temporal information from video inputs through our proposed 3D-wavelet gating and temporal-aware autoencoder, our method achieves 20% improvement in FID on the most challenging datasets over recent SOTA of image-based diffusion models. Meanwhile, due to the existence of rich temporal information, our method achieves strong performance while having 3times fewer parameters. Such a parameter reduction results in 2.3x speed up. By incorporating optical flow guidance, our method requires 9000x less training data and achieves over 20x fewer parameters than video-based diffusion models. Codes and results are available at our project page: https://zonglinl.github.io/tlbvfi_page.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TLB-VFI: Temporal-Aware Latent Brownian Bridge Diffusion for Video Frame Interpolation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TLB-VFI, a Temporal-Aware Latent Brownian Bridge Diffusion model for video frame interpolation. The method aims to address the limitations of existing diffusion-based VFI models, specifically regarding efficiency, model size, and the ability to effectively extract temporal information. TLB-VFI employs a temporal-aware autoencoder that extracts temporal information in both the latent space and pixel space through 3D-wavelet gating.  It uses Brownian Bridge Diffusion in the latent space and incorporates optical flow guidance for improved performance and efficiency. Experiments show that TLB-VFI achieves state-of-the-art performance with fewer parameters and a faster inference time compared to existing image-based and video-based diffusion models. A theoretical constraint regarding the effectiveness of Brownian Bridge diffusion is also proposed and validated.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has some novelty in its architecture and methodology.
    *   The use of both latent space and pixel space temporal information extraction using a 3D wavelet gating mechanism within an autoencoder is a relatively novel architectural choice.
    *   The theoretical analysis of when Brownian Bridge Diffusion is effective and its application within the VFI context contributes to the theoretical understanding of the method.
    *   Replacing the intermediate frame with zeros during inference, and leveraging the Brownian Bridge diffusion to restore the lost temporal information, is a clever idea.
    *   The way they adapted a standard Brownian Bridge diffusion model by zeroing out frames and diffusion in the latent space could be seen as incremental, but it does address a critical problem in VFI and diffusion models.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in improving the efficiency and performance of diffusion-based VFI methods.
    *   Achieving state-of-the-art performance with a smaller model and faster inference time makes diffusion models more practical for VFI.
    *   The demonstration of a 20% improvement in FID on challenging datasets like SNU-FILM-extreme and Xiph-4K over recent SOTAs suggests a substantial improvement in visual quality.
    *   Reducing the reliance on massive training datasets compared to other video diffusion models is significant in terms of resource requirements.
    *   The findings could influence the development of more efficient and practical diffusion-based models for video processing tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-structured approach to the VFI problem.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of TLB-VFI over existing methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   The paper includes a theoretical analysis of Brownian Bridge Diffusion's effectiveness.
    *   The efficiency gains (smaller model, faster inference) are significant.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on optical flow estimation could introduce potential error, and the paper could benefit from a deeper discussion on how robust TLB-VFI is to inaccuracies in the estimated optical flows.
    *   While the theoretical analysis is interesting, its practical impact could be limited if it only applies to very specific scenarios.
    *   While the authors mention potential acceleration of inference, details on hardware optimizations or deployment scenarios are limited.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of failure cases and limitations of the proposed method.
    *   The authors compare against a limited number of SOTA methods. While these are very strong baselines, comparison to other traditional (non-diffusion) based methods might provide more comprehensive context

*   **Overall Assessment:**
    The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of video frame interpolation. The proposed TLB-VFI model achieves a significant improvement in efficiency and performance over existing diffusion-based methods. While the reliance on optical flow and the limited scope of the theoretical analysis are weaknesses, the overall results and clear methodology make this a noteworthy advancement. The integration of 3D wavelet feature gating is also an interesting component. The claims made are supported by experimental evidence.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** While the paper isn't groundbreaking, it presents a strong, well-engineered, and efficient solution to a practical problem. The use of Brownian Bridge diffusion and 3D-wavelet feature gating are well motivated and lead to demonstrated improvements. However, there is room for improvement by analyzing the method's robustness to flow estimation errors, comparing against a wider range of baselines, and providing more insight into edge cases and practical limitations. The zeroing out of frames and diffusion in the latent space can be seen as incremental, but is effective and addresses a critical problem in VFI. Overall, the paper is of high quality and contributes positively to the video frame interpolation field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Autonomy to Agency: Agentic Vehicles for Human-Centered Mobility Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04996v1)
- **Authors**: Jiangbo Yu
- **Abstract**: Autonomy, from the Greek autos (self) and nomos (law), refers to the capacity to operate according to internal rules without external control. Accordingly, autonomous vehicles (AuVs) are defined as systems capable of perceiving their environment and executing preprogrammed tasks independently of external input. However, both research and real-world deployments increasingly showcase vehicles that demonstrate behaviors beyond this definition (including the SAE levels 1 to 6), such as interaction with humans and machines, goal adaptation, contextual reasoning, external tool use, and long-term planning, particularly with the integration of large language models (LLMs) and agentic AI systems. These developments reveal a conceptual gap between technical autonomy and the broader cognitive and social capabilities needed for future human-centered mobility systems. To address this, we introduce the concept of agentic vehicles (AgVs), referring to vehicles that integrate agentic AI to reason, adapt, and interact within complex environments. This paper presents a systems-level framework to characterize AgVs, focusing on their cognitive and communicative layers and differentiating them from conventional AuVs. It synthesizes relevant advances in agentic AI, robotics, multi-agent systems, and human-machine interaction, and highlights how agentic AI, through high-level reasoning and tool use, can function not merely as computational tools but as interactive agents embedded in mobility ecosystems. The paper concludes by identifying key challenges in the development and governance of AgVs, including safety, real-time control, public acceptance, ethical alignment, and regulatory frameworks.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, "From Autonomy to Agency: Agentic Vehicles for Human-Centered Mobility Systems."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the concept of "Agentic Vehicles" (AgVs) as a novel category of intelligent vehicles that surpass traditional autonomous vehicles (AuVs). While AuVs focus on independent operation based on pre-programmed rules, AgVs are characterized by their ability to reason, adapt, communicate, and learn within complex environments. The paper argues that the increasing integration of large language models (LLMs) and agentic AI systems necessitates a shift from the autonomy-centric view to an agency-focused approach. The paper proposes a systems-level framework for characterizing AgVs, emphasizing their cognitive and communicative layers. It then outlines a developmental framework with five levels of agency and discusses the broader implications and policy recommendations related to AgVs, including ethical considerations, societal impacts, and environmental dynamics. The paper concludes by highlighting the challenges and future research directions for the development and governance of AgVs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's core strength lies in its conceptual reframing of vehicle intelligence. Shifting the focus from "autonomy" to "agency" in the context of increasingly sophisticated AI integration is a valuable contribution. The introduction of the AgV concept and the articulation of its distinguishing features (goal adaptability, dialogic interaction, tool invocation, lifecycle intelligence) provides a novel lens for understanding the future of mobility. The developmental framework offers a practical way to assess the progress of AgV development.

* **Significance:**  The significance of this work comes from its ability to anticipate and proactively address the evolving landscape of intelligent transportation.  The paper highlights that, as vehicles gain sophisticated reasoning and communication capabilities, a new conceptual framework is needed to guide their development and governance. The discussion of ethical, societal, and environmental implications is particularly important.  The policy recommendations provide a starting point for shaping the future of AgVs in a responsible and beneficial way. The paper's synthesis of relevant research from agentic AI, robotics, and human-machine interaction is also a significant strength.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Conceptualization:** The paper clearly defines and differentiates between AuVs and AgVs, providing a solid foundation for further research.
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The proposed framework for characterizing AgVs covers technological, architectural, and behavioral aspects.
    *   **Proactive Policy Recommendations:** The paper anticipates potential challenges and offers concrete policy recommendations for responsible AgV development and deployment.
    *   **Multidisciplinary Approach:** The paper integrates insights from AI, robotics, transportation, ethics, and policy.
    *   **Timeliness:** Given the rapid advancements in LLMs and agentic AI, the paper's focus on the agency dimension is highly timely.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The paper is primarily conceptual and lacks empirical validation of the proposed framework and benefits of AgVs. While it's forward-looking, some concrete examples of AgVs in action or simulation would strengthen the arguments.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Technical Challenges:** While the paper mentions technical foundations (Generative AI, RL, etc.), it could delve deeper into the specific technical challenges of building and deploying AgVs, such as ensuring safety, reliability, and real-time performance.
    *   **Level of specificity**: While identifying high-level challenges, the paper could benefits from concrete policy recommendation and actionable steps that can be taken by government and researchers.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research and development in intelligent transportation systems by shifting the focus from pure automation to a more holistic view that considers agency, interaction, and ethical considerations. It can inform policy discussions and guide the development of standards and regulations for AgVs. It can also inspire new research directions in areas such as human-machine collaboration, ethical AI, and sustainable mobility.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and timely conceptual reframing of vehicle intelligence. However, its lack of empirical validation and limited discussion of technical challenges prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the policy recommendations are helpful, they remain somewhat general and could be more actionable. The significance of the paper is evident in its ability to anticipate the implications of emerging AI technologies and propose a proactive approach to shaping the future of mobility. However, the ideas are high level, and a next step would be to translate the core insights into quantifiable, measurable outcomes. Overall, the paper is a solid contribution to the field, but there remains a way for it to have more specific and concrete impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### Co-DETECT: Collaborative Discovery of Edge Cases in Text Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05010v1)
- **Authors**: Chenfei Xiong, Jingwei Ni, Yu Fan, Vilém Zouhar, Donya Rooein, Lorena Calvo-Bartolomé, Alexander Hoyle, Zhijing Jin, Mrinmaya Sachan, Markus Leippold, Dirk Hovy, Mennatallah El-Assady, Elliott Ash
- **Abstract**: We introduce Co-DETECT (Collaborative Discovery of Edge cases in TExt ClassificaTion), a novel mixed-initiative annotation framework that integrates human expertise with automatic annotation guided by large language models (LLMs). Co-DETECT starts with an initial, sketch-level codebook and dataset provided by a domain expert, then leverages the LLM to annotate the data and identify edge cases that are not well described by the initial codebook. Specifically, Co-DETECT flags challenging examples, induces high-level, generalizable descriptions of edge cases, and assists user in incorporating edge case handling rules to improve the codebook. This iterative process enables more effective handling of nuanced phenomena through compact, generalizable annotation rules. Extensive user study, qualitative and quantitative analyses prove the effectiveness of Co-DETECT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Co-DETECT, a novel mixed-initiative annotation framework designed to improve text classification by facilitating collaborative discovery of edge cases. It combines human expertise with large language model (LLM) capabilities. Co-DETECT starts with an initial codebook and dataset provided by a domain expert. The LLM annotates data, identifies edge cases that the initial codebook fails to adequately address, generates high-level descriptions of these edge cases, and assists in incorporating edge case handling rules into the codebook. This iterative process aims to enable more effective handling of nuanced phenomena. The paper presents user study results, both qualitative and quantitative, to demonstrate Co-DETECT's effectiveness. The system is intended to support domain experts in situations where data annotation requires domain expertise and where both codebook development and data annotation are human effort intensive.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the integration of an LLM into the codebook development process in a systematic, iterative, and expert-in-the-loop manner. Previous work has explored LLMs for annotation, but Co-DETECT focuses on *codebook refinement and edge case detection*, which is a less explored area. The specific workflow, including the use of LLMs for identification, clustering, and rule generation related to edge cases, is a significant contribution. While leveraging LLMs for annotation is not new, the *focused approach to edge case management and codebook improvement is*.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem in text classification: the inherent difficulty of creating comprehensive and reliable codebooks, especially when dealing with nuanced or subjective phenomena. Overlooking edge cases can lead to inaccurate models and potentially unfair or biased outcomes. Co-DETECT’s ability to surface such cases and enable domain experts to incorporate them into the annotation guidelines has the potential to substantially improve the quality and robustness of text classification systems. The impact on research involving hate speech detection, sentiment analysis, and other subjective classification tasks is potentially high. The system addresses the inherent limitations domain experts have in explicating tacit knowledge related to edge cases and, consequently, helps improve automation.
    *   **Strengths:** The paper's strengths lie in the well-defined problem, a clear description of the Co-DETECT framework, a sound evaluation methodology (user studies with domain experts, quantitative assessment of edge case validity), and positive results showing that Co-DETECT can identify relevant edge cases and improve codebook quality. The paper includes frontend implementation details, contributing to reproducibility.

    *   **Weaknesses:** The paper could be improved by providing more details on the specific prompts used with the LLMs (including the reasoning LLM) for edge case detection, description generation, and rule creation. A comprehensive analysis of the *types* of edge cases Co-DETECT is particularly good or bad at identifying would strengthen the paper. While the paper claims to address Polanyi's paradox, a discussion on the limitations of how well the paradox is resolved by the system would also strengthen the paper. The scale of the user study is limited to 10 participants which may impact the generalization of results. Also, while improving LLM annotation with improved codebook is shown, it's unclear the extent improved automatic annotation using LLMs reduce the need for Co-DETECT.
    *   **Impact:** The work has the potential to influence how text classification projects are initiated and maintained, particularly those involving complex or sensitive topics where edge cases are common. It could lead to the development of more robust and fair models and better integration of human expertise with AI-assisted annotation workflows. The release of the system may also foster more research in the area of codebook creation and edge case management.

* **Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by addressing the challenging problem of edge case management in text classification through an innovative expert-in-the-loop framework leveraging LLMs. The system and results presented demonstrate promise in improving the quality of annotations. The work, however, has limitations related to the scale of the user study and details on specific LLM implementations. Considering its contribution and remaining scope for improvement, a score of 7 appropriately captures the paper's value.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Meta-Learning Transformers to Improve In-Context Generalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05019v1)
- **Authors**: Lorenzo Braccaioli, Anna Vettoruzzo, Prabhant Singh, Joaquin Vanschoren, Mohamed-Rafik Bouguelia, Nicola Conci
- **Abstract**: In-context learning enables transformer models to generalize to new tasks based solely on input prompts, without any need for weight updates. However, existing training paradigms typically rely on large, unstructured datasets that are costly to store, difficult to evaluate for quality and balance, and pose privacy and ethical concerns due to the inclusion of sensitive information. Motivated by these limitations and risks, we propose an alternative training strategy where we leverage a collection of multiple, small-scale, and domain-specific datasets. We empirically demonstrate that the increased quality and diversity of such data improve the generalization abilities of in-context learners beyond their training domain, while achieving comparable performance with models trained on a single large-scale dataset. We investigate this paradigm by leveraging meta-learning to train an in-context learner on the Meta-Album collection under several settings. Firstly, we show the performance in a controlled environment, where the test domain is completely excluded from the training knowledge. Secondly, we explore the robustness of these models to forgetting in a continual scenario where the information is accessible for a limited time. Finally, we explore the more challenging unsupervised scenario. Our findings demonstrate that transformers still generalize for in-context prediction when trained on a curated dataset collection while offering advantages in modularity and replaceability.
- **Summary**: This paper proposes a meta-learning approach, GEOM, to train transformers for in-context learning using multiple smaller, domain-specific datasets instead of large, unstructured datasets. The authors argue that this offers advantages in terms of data quality control, diversity, privacy, and modularity. They evaluate GEOM under three different training scenarios: supervised (offline), sequential (lifelong learning with curriculum learning), and unsupervised. The results demonstrate that GEOM achieves comparable or even better generalization abilities than models trained on a single large-scale dataset, especially in sequential and unsupervised settings, while offering improved modularity and replaceability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using meta-learning with transformers on smaller, curated datasets to achieve in-context generalization is moderately novel. While previous work has explored meta-learning in conjunction with in-context learning, the specific focus on leveraging domain-specific datasets and the detailed analysis of different training pipelines (supervised, sequential, unsupervised) adds some incremental novelty. The non-causal approach to sequence modelling has been explored previously, so its use here is an application rather than an innovation.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a relevant issue: the limitations and ethical concerns associated with training large models on massive, uncurated datasets. Proposing a viable alternative that leverages smaller, more manageable datasets is significant. The experiments demonstrate that this approach can be competitive with large-scale pre-training, which is a valuable contribution to the field. The insights into the impact of data diversity, curriculum learning, and the number of classes on generalization are also significant. However, the performance gains are not consistently overwhelming, and the approach may require careful tuning of the datasets used.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined experimental setup with clear training pipelines and baselines.
    *   Thorough evaluation across different learning scenarios (supervised, sequential, unsupervised).
    *   Interesting insights into the factors influencing generalization, such as data diversity and curriculum learning.
    *   Practical relevance in addressing the limitations of large-scale pre-training.
    * Rigorous testing involving the use of curriculum-based learning, which demonstrates the advantages of structured task sequencing.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance improvements over large-scale pre-training are not consistently dramatic. In some cases, it only matches what previous methods did.
    *   The approach may require careful curation and selection of domain-specific datasets, which could be challenging in practice.
    * The method heavily relies on an established architecture without introducing any particularly novel components of its own.
    * Lacks extensive detail on the specific types and configurations of data augmentation used, diminishing the replicability of the research to a certain extent.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the direction of research towards more sustainable and ethical training paradigms for in-context learning. It could also encourage the development of tools and techniques for curating and managing domain-specific datasets for meta-learning. The demonstration of effective in-context generalization from a curated dataset collection provides an advantage over training models with large amounts of unstructured, and potentially sensitive, data.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a valuable exploration of an alternative training strategy for in-context learning that addresses the limitations of large-scale pre-training. The thorough experimental evaluation and the interesting insights into data diversity and curriculum learning contribute to the field. The performance gains are not transformative, and there are challenges associated with dataset curation. The approach is practical and demonstrates effective generalization but builds on existing ideas without introducing significant architectural innovation. Overall, it offers a valuable contribution to the ongoing development of sustainable learning paradigms.

### A Generative Diffusion Model for Amorphous Materials
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05024v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Yang, Daniel Schwalbe-Koda
- **Abstract**: Generative models show great promise for the inverse design of molecules and inorganic crystals, but remain largely ineffective within more complex structures such as amorphous materials. Here, we present a diffusion model that reliably generates amorphous structures up to 1000 times faster than conventional simulations across processing conditions, compositions, and data sources. Generated structures recovered the short- and medium-range order, sampling diversity, and macroscopic properties of silica glass, as validated by simulations and an information-theoretical strategy. Conditional generation allowed sampling large structures at low cooling rates of 10$^{-2}$ K/ps to uncover a ductile-to-brittle transition and mesoporous silica structures. Extension to metallic glassy systems accurately reproduced local structures and properties from both computational and experimental datasets, demonstrating how synthetic data can be generated from characterization results. Our methods provide a roadmap for the design and simulation of amorphous materials previously inaccessible to computational methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a generative diffusion model for creating amorphous material structures. The model is trained to denoise atomistic environments in a manner similar to denoising diffusion probabilistic models (DDPMs). The key contributions are: 1) demonstrating that the model can reliably generate amorphous structures orders of magnitude faster than traditional simulations, 2) showcasing the model's ability to capture short- and medium-range order and macroscopic properties of silica glass, validated through simulations and information-theoretical analysis, 3) enabling conditional generation of structures based on processing parameters like cooling rate, which reveals a ductile-to-brittle transition and allows for the creation of mesoporous silica structures, and 4) accurately reproducing structures and properties of metallic glassy systems from both computational and experimental data, highlighting the potential for synthetic data generation.  The authors demonstrate the model’s application across different compositions, densities, and processing conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of diffusion models to generate *amorphous* materials is a significant step beyond previous work focusing on crystalline structures. Prior generative models for amorphous materials struggled to create physically realistic samples or accurately represent macroscopic properties.  The authors address these shortcomings by introducing noise to escape local minima and developing a rigorous validation framework that includes metrics for structural validity, macroscopic properties, and novelty. Capturing experimental data adds to its importance.

*   **Significance:**  The potential impact on materials science is substantial. The acceleration of amorphous structure generation by orders of magnitude, especially when combined with the ability to condition the generation on synthesis parameters, opens up new avenues for materials design and simulation.  The ability to generate large-scale structures at low cooling rates, which are computationally prohibitive with conventional methods, could significantly improve the accuracy of simulations. It holds significant promise for experimental synthesis. The ability to augment characterization datasets with synthetic data can be a major step for computational models and also greatly speed experimental work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Rigorous Validation: A clear strength is the multi-faceted validation strategy, encompassing structural, mechanical, and information-theoretic metrics.
    *   Broad Applicability: The model is successfully applied to both silica glass and metallic glasses, demonstrating its versatility.
    *   Conditional Generation: Conditioning on cooling rates allows for exploring synthesis-structure relationships that are typically difficult to access.
    *   Speed: The speedup compared to traditional simulations is substantial and makes previously infeasible calculations possible.
    *   Augmenting experimental work is an innovative idea.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Still reliant on MD data: The model is trained on data generated through conventional simulations, limiting its scope to potentials available. It will need to handle potential problems in cases where the models are worse. The method to refine generated structure relies on MD simulation as well.
    *   Black Box: The model is inherently a "black box," making it challenging to directly understand the underlying relationships it learns.
    *   Yield Strengths still an issue: the fact that this metric still underperforms shows there is still room to grow.

*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   Accelerated Materials Design: Facilitates faster exploration of amorphous material compositions and processing conditions.
    *   Improved Simulation Accuracy: Allows for simulations at more realistic cooling rates and larger scales, leading to more accurate predictions.
    *   Data Augmentation: Augments experimental data to improve material analysis.

*   **Conclusion:**
    This paper presents a novel and significant advance in the application of generative models to amorphous materials. The authors have successfully addressed key limitations of previous methods by generating physically realistic structures that can accurately reproduce structural, mechanical, and thermal properties. The demonstrated ability to condition these models to experimental results makes the method much more practical and scalable.

Score: 8.5

- **Score**: 8/10

### Estimating Object Physical Properties from RGB-D Vision and Depth Robot Sensors Using Deep Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05029v1)
- **Authors**: Ricardo Cardoso, Plinio Moreno
- **Abstract**: Inertial mass plays a crucial role in robotic applications such as object grasping, manipulation, and simulation, providing a strong prior for planning and control. Accurately estimating an object's mass before interaction can significantly enhance the performance of various robotic tasks. However, mass estimation using only vision sensors is a relatively underexplored area. This paper proposes a novel approach combining sparse point-cloud data from depth images with RGB images to estimate the mass of objects. We evaluate a range of point-cloud processing architectures, alongside RGB-only methods. To overcome the limited availability of training data, we create a synthetic dataset using ShapeNetSem 3D models, simulating RGBD images via a Kinect camera. This synthetic data is used to train an image generation model for estimating dense depth maps, which we then use to augment an existing dataset of images paired with mass values. Our approach significantly outperforms existing benchmarks across all evaluated metrics. The data generation (https://github.com/RavineWindteer/ShapenetSem-to-RGBD) as well as the training of the depth estimator (https://github.com/RavineWindteer/GLPDepth-Edited) and the mass estimator (https://github.com/RavineWindteer/Depth-mass-estimator) are available online.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary, critical evaluation, and score for the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of estimating the mass of objects using visual information, specifically RGB-D data. The authors argue that accurate mass estimation is crucial for robotic applications like grasping and manipulation, and vision-based approaches offer advantages over physical interaction before grasping.  The core of the work is augmenting an existing RGB-mass dataset (image2mass) with depth information. This is achieved by training a GLPDepth model on a synthetic dataset generated from ShapeNetSem 3D models to predict depth maps from RGB images. The trained depth prediction model is then used to generate depth maps for the image2mass dataset. Finally, the authors propose a multimodal architecture that takes an RGB image and a sparse point cloud (derived from the predicted depth map) as input to estimate the object's mass. The paper explores several variations of the architecture, including different point cloud encoders (PointNet, DGCNN, PointTransformer) and a reconstruction task. The results demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms existing benchmarks on the image2mass dataset, and that using depth significantly improves the mass estimation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates moderate novelty. The idea of using depth information to augment RGB data for mass estimation is logical and not entirely new (previous works used RGB-D in specific domains). However, the authors' approach is novel in two main aspects:
    *   Fine-tuning a state-of-the-art depth estimation model (GLPDepth) on a synthetic dataset specifically designed to mimic the image2mass dataset's characteristics. This targeted fine-tuning to generate depth information for an existing mass dataset distinguishes the approach.
    *   A comprehensive evaluation of different point cloud encoding architectures (PointNet, DGCNN, PointTransformer) within a multimodal RGB-D mass estimation framework. This systematic comparison of encoder architectures is also a novel element.
    *   Testing the incorporation of point cloud reconstruction as an auxiliary task.

*   **Significance:** The work has a good significance for the robotics and computer vision communities.
    *   It addresses an important problem in robotics—mass estimation—which is vital for planning, control, and safe manipulation.
    *   It tackles the limited availability of RGB-D mass datasets by proposing a practical data augmentation strategy using synthetic data and pre-trained depth estimation models. This approach is likely to be useful for other tasks where labeled datasets are scarce.
    *   The performance gains compared to existing benchmarks on the image2mass dataset are significant, demonstrating the value of incorporating depth information.
    *   The evaluation of different point cloud encoders provides valuable insights into their suitability for this task.
    *   It's disappointing that the point cloud reconstruction task didn't lead to significant improvements. It raises interesting questions about the relationships between the two tasks, and may potentially spur further investigations in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to follow.
    *   The approach is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The experimental setup is rigorous, with a comprehensive evaluation of different architectures and data augmentation strategies.
    *   The results are clearly presented and demonstrate significant performance improvements over existing methods.
    *   The authors make their code and datasets available, which promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   Good analysis of the results and limitations of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on synthetic data for depth prediction could limit the generalizability to real-world scenarios, especially those with different lighting conditions or object textures. The authors acknowledge this limitation, but further investigation of robustness to real-world noise and variability would be valuable.
    *   While the paper evaluates different point cloud encoders, the depth maps themselves are predicted, not directly measured. This adds a layer of uncertainty to the input data. It would be beneficial to explore the performance of the method with real RGB-D data, if available.
    *   The study investigates several point cloud encoders, but these choices are also specific and could be further optimized for this particular mass estimation task. More specifically, it is noted that more complex architectures performed worse than a simple PointNet architecture. In order to more comprehensively evaluate the models, the authors should have conducted more ablation studies with different hyperparameters for all the architectures they tested.
    *   Lack of comparison against other RGB-D methods. While they outperform RGB methods, a direct comparison of their approach with other works explicitly designing estimators for RGB-D sensors would give better insights.
    *   The authors did not conduct a more in-depth analysis of why the different architectures were underperforming. It seems like the method could be further optimized if the authors were able to isolate specifically what was causing the models to underperform.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in robotic perception, manipulation, and computer vision. The proposed data augmentation strategy and the insights gained from the evaluation of different point cloud encoders could be valuable to other researchers working on similar tasks.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of mass estimation by demonstrating the benefits of incorporating depth information into existing RGB-based approaches. The proposed data augmentation strategy and the systematic evaluation of different point cloud encoders are valuable contributions. The performance improvements over existing benchmarks are significant. However, the reliance on synthetic depth data and a relatively limited evaluation of real-world performance prevent a higher score. Furthermore, the lack of ablation studies prevents the reader from isolating specific reasons why models underperform and prevents more comprehensive hyperparameter selection.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MoLink: Distributed and Efficient Serving Framework for Large Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05043v1)
- **Authors**: Lewei Jin, Yongqi Chen, Kui Zhang, Yifan Zhuo, Yi Gao, Bowei Yang, Zhengong Cai, Wei Dong
- **Abstract**: Large language models represent a groundbreaking shift in generative AI. Yet, these advances come with a significant challenge: the high cost of model serving. To mitigate these costs, consumer-grade GPUs emerge as a more affordable alternative. This presents an opportunity for more cost-efficient LLM serving by leveraging these GPUs. However, it is non-trivial to achieve high-efficiency LLM serving on consumer-grade GPUs, mainly due to two challenges: 1) these GPUs are often deployed in limited network conditions; 2) these GPUs often exhibit heterogeneity in host systems. To address these challenges, we present MoLink, a distributed LLM serving system for large models. It incorporates several key techniques, enabling efficient LLM serving on heterogeneous and weakly connected consumer-grade GPUs. Our experiments demonstrate that it achieves throughput improvements of up to 458\% and cost-profit margin improvements of up to 151\%, compared to state-of-the-art systems. MoLink allows users on Windows, Linux, and containerized VMs to seamlessly integrate GPUs with just a few lines of code over Ethernet or public networks. Currently, it supports 18 mainstream architectures of open-source large language models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've read the paper and I'm ready to provide a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MoLink, a distributed and efficient serving framework for large language models (LLMs) designed to leverage consumer-grade GPUs. The authors address the challenges of using these GPUs, namely limited network bandwidth and heterogeneity in host systems (Windows, Linux, VMs). MoLink incorporates techniques like dynamic micro-batch scheduling and chunk transmission to improve throughput and cost-efficiency. Experiments show improvements in throughput and cost-profit margin compared to existing systems like Petals and vLLM. The system supports various open-source LLM architectures and integrates with different operating systems and virtualization environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines existing techniques (pipeline parallelism, micro-batching) with optimizations tailored for consumer-grade GPUs and weak network conditions. While the individual techniques aren't groundbreaking, the specific combination and application to this niche area (distributed LLM serving on heterogeneous, low-bandwidth systems) offers some novelty. The dynamic micro-batch scheduling and chunk transmission optimizations appear to be the most innovative aspects, but the technical details remain at a high level. It is not clear how the specific algorithms for "chunking" and "dynamic micro-batching" are implemented and how well they adapt to different scenarios.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly relevant. The cost of serving LLMs is a significant barrier to wider adoption, and using consumer-grade GPUs offers a potential solution. MoLink tackles practical challenges in this area, such as network limitations and system heterogeneity. The experimental results, showing improvements over existing systems, are promising. The claim of supporting "18 mainstream architectures" is impressive and could lead to broader usability if truly seamless. However, some areas of potential concern are the claims regarding improvements in the "cost-profit margin" and how that translates into real-world use case scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a relevant and timely problem.
    *   Provides a practical system with demonstrable improvements.
    *   Supports heterogeneous environments and a variety of LLM architectures.
    *   Clear articulation of the challenges with consumer-grade GPUs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited technical detail regarding the core optimizations (dynamic micro-batching, chunk transmission). The paper lacks a deeper dive into these algorithmic details. The description remains at a conceptual level.
