Using simple prompts for instruction tuning can be considered a slight positive, but overall not a significant one, unless the specific prompt engineering revealed something surprising, which is not the case here. Also, in the task of identifying the origin of the text, it is a standard approach, the evaluation of different models and prompt engineering is a usual procedure.

* **Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate.  Improved AI-generated text detection remains an important problem, particularly with the increasing sophistication and misuse of LLMs. The paper contributes to this field by providing empirical results on specific models and datasets, which can be useful for benchmarking and comparison. However, the relatively low accuracy achieved on Task B highlights the challenges in model attribution.  The observed limitations of the GPT_40-mini due to content filtering, and the limited generalization of BERT and LLaMA-3 8B on the test dataset, provide useful insights, but the paper doesn't propose a significant breakthrough. The insights obtained may also not translate to other models or datasets, limiting the broader impact. There is no new architecture being proposed, so the overall significance for the advancement of this field is limited.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem definition and experimental setup.
    * Empirical results on relevant models and datasets.
    * Analysis of model limitations and insights into the challenges of AI-generated text detection, especially model attribution.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited novelty in methodology.
    * Lower accuracy on the more challenging model attribution task (Task B).
    * Lack of deeper analysis of _why_ certain models performed better or worse (e.g., an analysis of the types of errors made by each model).
    * No exploration of more sophisticated instruction tuning techniques or prompt engineering strategies.
    * The results on GPT_4-o were affected by content filtering, which affects the comparability of the results.
    *  Lack of comparison with other state-of-the-art methods for AI-generated text detection. The paper mentions some of these techniques, but lacks a discussion on why it uses these specific models instead of others.

* **Impact and Influence:**
    The paper is unlikely to have a major impact on the field.  While it adds to the body of knowledge on AI-generated text detection, it doesn't present a fundamentally new approach or achieve significantly better results than existing methods.  Its potential influence will be limited to serving as a reference point for future benchmarking and comparison of models and datasets. The lack of insights on _why_ certain models perform better and more complex instructions limit the influence of the work.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **5** to this paper. The paper addresses an important problem, presents a clear experimental methodology, and provides some empirical results. However, it lacks significant novelty and doesn't achieve groundbreaking results. The limitations in model attribution accuracy and the absence of deeper analysis weaken its overall contribution. While a solid effort, it falls short of being a highly influential paper in the field.
Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### OpenS2S: Advancing Open-Source End-to-End Empathetic Large Speech Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05177v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Wang, Tianyu Peng, Wen Yang, Yinan Bai, Guangfu Wang, Jun Lin, Lanpeng Jia, Lingxiang Wu, Jinqiao Wang, Chengqing Zong, Jiajun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Empathetic interaction is a cornerstone of human-machine communication, due to the need for understanding speech enriched with paralinguistic cues and generating emotional and expressive responses. However, the most powerful empathetic LSLMs are increasingly closed off, leaving the crucial details about the architecture, data and development opaque to researchers. Given the critical need for transparent research into the LSLMs and empathetic behavior, we present OpenS2S, a fully open-source, transparent and end-to-end LSLM designed to enable empathetic speech interactions. Based on our empathetic speech-to-text model BLSP-Emo, OpenS2S further employs a streaming interleaved decoding architecture to achieve low-latency speech generation. To facilitate end-to-end training, OpenS2S incorporates an automated data construction pipeline that synthesizes diverse, high-quality empathetic speech dialogues at low cost. By leveraging large language models to generate empathetic content and controllable text-to-speech systems to introduce speaker and emotional variation, we construct a scalable training corpus with rich paralinguistic diversity and minimal human supervision. We release the fully open-source OpenS2S model, including the dataset, model weights, pre-training and fine-tuning codes, to empower the broader research community and accelerate innovation in empathetic speech systems. The project webpage can be accessed at https://casia-lm.github.io/OpenS2S
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "OpenS2S : Advancing Fully Open-Source End-to-End Empathetic Large Speech Language Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OpenS2S, a fully open-source, end-to-end Large Speech Language Model (LSLM) designed to facilitate empathetic speech interactions. The model builds upon the existing BLSP-Emo speech-to-text model and incorporates a streaming interleaved decoding architecture for low-latency speech generation.  A key contribution is the automated data construction pipeline, which synthesizes diverse and high-quality empathetic speech dialogues using LLMs for empathetic content generation and controllable text-to-speech (TTS) systems to introduce speaker and emotional variations. The authors release the complete model, dataset, and code to promote transparent research in empathetic speech systems. The work also focuses on training techniques to improve the empathetic abilities of the model, including techniques that enable more nuanced, and semantically relevant, empathetic responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the comprehensive open-source release of an empathetic LSLM, along with the associated dataset and training pipeline. While individual components (e.g., interleaved decoding, using LLMs for data augmentation, emotional TTS) are not entirely new, the combination and the emphasis on full transparency and reproducibility are significant. The automated empathetic data generation pipeline seems like a practical and scalable approach to address the data scarcity problem. The approach of combining publicly available tools and models to create a high quality empathetic LSLM is novel in itself.

*   **Significance:** The significance is multi-fold:

    *   **Democratization of Research:** The open-source nature breaks down the barrier to entry for researchers who lack the resources to train large proprietary models or acquire massive datasets. This allows for more widespread exploration of empathetic AI, including analysis of biases, cultural variations, and ethical implications.
    *   **Reproducibility and Extensibility:**  OpenS2S enables researchers to understand the inner workings of the model, replicate the results, and build upon the architecture for further innovation.
    *   **Practical Application:** The model, as is, provides a foundation for building more natural and human-centered human-machine interfaces. The focus on low-latency streaming is particularly relevant for real-time conversational agents.
    *   **Focus on Empathetic Response:** The system is focused on not just general dialogue or speech response, but more nuanced empathetic reactions. This shows focus on more involved interaction.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Full Open-Source Release:** The most significant strength is the full release of the code, model, data, and training procedures.
    *   **Data Augmentation Pipeline:** The automated pipeline is a creative and practical solution to the data bottleneck in empathetic AI.
    *   **End-to-End Design:** The end-to-end architecture streamlines development and deployment.
    *   **Emphasis on Streaming:** The streaming architecture is key for real-time applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Components:** The model is primarily built on existing building blocks (Qwen2, Whisper, etc.). While this facilitates development, the core algorithmic contributions are somewhat limited.
    *   **Dependency on LLM Quality:** The quality of the generated data is heavily dependent on the performance of the LLM used in the data augmentation pipeline. Biases or limitations in the LLM could propagate to the training data.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation, while sound, seems relatively basic. More rigorous analysis of the empathetic qualities of the model (e.g., human evaluation) would further strengthen the claims. It is also unclear how well OpenS2S deals with complex or nuanced situations in an empathetic manner.
    *   **Potential for Misuse:** The capability to generate speech with emotional expressiveness carries the risk of misuse for malicious purposes (e.g., generating emotionally manipulative fake audio). The paper does not explicitly address these ethical concerns.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by providing a strong baseline for empathetic speech AI and lowering the barrier to entry for research. It encourages researchers to focus on more nuanced aspects of empathy and to analyze and mitigate biases in these systems. The approach could inspire other researchers to adopt similar data augmentation pipelines for their models.

**Justification for Score:**

While the core algorithmic innovation might be incremental, the combination of techniques, the focus on empathy, and, most importantly, the *complete open-source release* warrant a high score.  The potential to democratize research and foster innovation in this crucial area of AI is substantial. The automated data augmentation pipeline is also a valuable contribution. However, the dependence on pre-existing components and the potential for misuse temper the enthusiasm. The model seems like a set of well-integrated models with a few changes, rather than a ground breaking new discovery. This hurts the score. There were also issues regarding the evaluation and testing of the system. A score of 7 is a reasonable placement for this paper.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EmbodieDreamer: Advancing Real2Sim2Real Transfer for Policy Training via Embodied World Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05198v1)
- **Authors**: Boyuan Wang, Xinpan Meng, Xiaofeng Wang, Zheng Zhu, Angen Ye, Yang Wang, Zhiqin Yang, Chaojun Ni, Guan Huang, Xingang Wang
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of Embodied AI has led to an increasing demand for large-scale, high-quality real-world data. However, collecting such embodied data remains costly and inefficient. As a result, simulation environments have become a crucial surrogate for training robot policies. Yet, the significant Real2Sim2Real gap remains a critical bottleneck, particularly in terms of physical dynamics and visual appearance. To address this challenge, we propose EmbodieDreamer, a novel framework that reduces the Real2Sim2Real gap from both the physics and appearance perspectives. Specifically, we propose PhysAligner, a differentiable physics module designed to reduce the Real2Sim physical gap. It jointly optimizes robot-specific parameters such as control gains and friction coefficients to better align simulated dynamics with real-world observations. In addition, we introduce VisAligner, which incorporates a conditional video diffusion model to bridge the Sim2Real appearance gap by translating low-fidelity simulated renderings into photorealistic videos conditioned on simulation states, enabling high-fidelity visual transfer. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of EmbodieDreamer. The proposed PhysAligner reduces physical parameter estimation error by 3.74% compared to simulated annealing methods while improving optimization speed by 89.91\%. Moreover, training robot policies in the generated photorealistic environment leads to a 29.17% improvement in the average task success rate across real-world tasks after reinforcement learning. Code, model and data will be publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "EmbodieDreamer: Advancing Real2Sim2Real Transfer for Policy Training via Embodied World Modeling" proposes a new framework called EmbodieDreamer to reduce the Real2Sim2Real gap in robot policy training. The framework consists of two main components: PhysAligner, a differentiable physics module that optimizes robot-specific parameters to align simulated dynamics with real-world observations, and VisAligner, a conditional video diffusion model that translates low-fidelity simulated renderings into photorealistic videos conditioned on simulation states.  The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that PhysAligner improves physical parameter estimation accuracy and optimization speed, while VisAligner generates photorealistic environments that lead to better task success rates in real-world tasks after reinforcement learning. The framework also shows improvements in imitation learning when the generated environment is used for data augmentation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has decent novelty. While the individual components, differentiable physics optimization and conditional video generation, are not entirely novel, the *integration* of these techniques within a single framework specifically designed to address both physical and visual aspects of the Real2Sim2Real gap is a significant contribution. The disentangled conditioning within VisAligner (separating robot, foreground, and background) offers a nuanced approach compared to existing methods.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a very important problem in robotics: the Real2Sim2Real gap. Overcoming this gap is crucial for effectively training robot policies in simulation and deploying them in the real world. The experimental results, particularly the improvement in real-world task success rates, demonstrate the practical significance of the proposed framework. The speedup in parameter estimation provided by PhysAligner is also valuable. The improvements through data augmentation via VisAligner also add another dimension of significance to the paper.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed framework and its components.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach on multiple tasks and benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component (PhysAligner and VisAligner).
    *   The use of differentiable physics offers a more efficient alternative to traditional methods like simulated annealing.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the limitations of the approach. For example, how well does it generalize to more complex and unstructured environments? Also, the computational cost of the diffusion-based video generation could be a limiting factor.
    *   The connection between the simulation, PhysAligner, VisAligner, and then policy training might be a bit complex to set up. Simpler is often better.
    *   The quantitative results could be more detailed. For instance, it would be useful to see the specific values of the physical parameters that were estimated.
    *   The dependence on manually calibrated camera parameters is a potential bottleneck. It would be desirable to have a more automated or robust approach to camera calibration.
*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in Real2Sim2Real transfer and robot policy training. The EmbodieDreamer framework provides a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners who want to train more realistic and effective robot policies in simulation. The use of differentiable physics and conditional video generation could also inspire new approaches to addressing the Real2Sim2Real gap. The results concerning data augmentation are also compelling and relevant.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper has clear strengths and addresses an important problem with a well-engineered solution, the fact that each element (physics optimization, video generation) isn't *completely* novel on its own, and that setup could be slightly complex, prevents it from achieving a higher score. The experimental validation is strong, and the potential for impact is considerable. Also, while real world performance improves, the single view performance *starts* lower than a simpler model (SFT with 3 views), indicating that more work may be needed in setting up the visual pipeline. However, the comprehensive integration of these components, the focus on addressing both physical and visual aspects, and the demonstrated improvements in real-world task performance make it a solid contribution.

### All in One: Visual-Description-Guided Unified Point Cloud Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05211v1)
- **Authors**: Zongyan Han, Mohamed El Amine Boudjoghra, Jiahua Dong, Jinhong Wang, Rao Muhammad Anwer
- **Abstract**: Unified segmentation of 3D point clouds is crucial for scene understanding, but is hindered by its sparse structure, limited annotations, and the challenge of distinguishing fine-grained object classes in complex environments. Existing methods often struggle to capture rich semantic and contextual information due to limited supervision and a lack of diverse multimodal cues, leading to suboptimal differentiation of classes and instances. To address these challenges, we propose VDG-Uni3DSeg, a novel framework that integrates pre-trained vision-language models (e.g., CLIP) and large language models (LLMs) to enhance 3D segmentation. By leveraging LLM-generated textual descriptions and reference images from the internet, our method incorporates rich multimodal cues, facilitating fine-grained class and instance separation. We further design a Semantic-Visual Contrastive Loss to align point features with multimodal queries and a Spatial Enhanced Module to model scene-wide relationships efficiently. Operating within a closed-set paradigm that utilizes multimodal knowledge generated offline, VDG-Uni3DSeg achieves state-of-the-art results in semantic, instance, and panoptic segmentation, offering a scalable and practical solution for 3D understanding. Our code is available at https://github.com/Hanzy1996/VDG-Uni3DSeg.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VDG-Uni3DSeg, a new framework for unified 3D point cloud segmentation. It leverages pre-trained vision-language models (VLMs) and large language models (LLMs) to enhance semantic, instance, and panoptic segmentation. The approach uses LLMs to generate detailed semantic descriptions of 3D object classes and retrieves class-specific reference images from the internet. These textual descriptions and images are encoded using CLIP and fused with point-wise features within a unified mask decoder.  A Semantic-Visual Contrastive (SVC) loss is introduced to align point features with textual and visual embeddings.  A Spatial Enhancement Module is also incorporated to capture scene-wide relationships efficiently. The method operates in a closed-set paradigm, using offline-generated multimodal knowledge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of LLM-generated textual descriptions and internet-sourced images into a unified 3D segmentation framework.  Prior works have explored multimodal 3D segmentation, but VDG-Uni3DSeg's approach to leverage LLMs for generating fine-grained semantic knowledge *without* needing paired 2D images and 3D point clouds is a key differentiator. The design of the Semantic-Visual Contrastive loss to align point features with both textual and visual embeddings is also a novel contribution.

* **Significance:** The significance of the work is that it addresses the limitations of existing 3D segmentation methods that often struggle with fine-grained object classes and limited annotations. By incorporating external knowledge from pre-trained LLMs and VLMs, the method improves class and instance differentiation.  The method's closed-set paradigm, avoids the computational overhead of real-time 2D-3D alignment, making it more practical for deployment. Quantitative and qualitative results demonstrate improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods on standard datasets (S3DIS, ScanNet, ScanNet200), showing gains in instance, semantic, and panoptic segmentation tasks. The ablation study helps demonstrate the contribution of each component.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The use of LLMs to generate semantic descriptions reduces the dependence on manual annotations.
    *   The SVC loss encourages better alignment of point features with relevant semantic and visual cues.
    *   The Spatial Enhancement Module efficiently captures scene-wide relationships.
    *   The closed-set paradigm makes the approach computationally efficient.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on multiple datasets.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   While a closed-set paradigm is efficient, it limits the method's ability to generalize to unseen object classes (open vocabulary).
    *   The reliance on the quality of LLM-generated descriptions and internet-sourced images might be a potential bottleneck. If the LLM provides poor descriptions, or if the internet search yields noisy images, performance could suffer. The paper shows a slight experiment with random images but a more thorough analysis of this sensitivity could be beneficial.
    *   The choice of specific LLMs (LLaMA) and VLMs (CLIP) may limit the approach's broader applicability. While these are strong models, showing results with different models would further validate the generalizability.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more thorough analysis of the computational cost and memory requirements of the proposed method.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper presents a promising direction for 3D segmentation by leveraging external knowledge from VLMs and LLMs. It's likely to influence future research in this area, particularly in improving the robustness and efficiency of multimodal 3D segmentation. The proposed SVC loss and the modular design could inspire new techniques for feature alignment and scene understanding.

* **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to 3D segmentation, there are a few limitations. The closed-set constraint, potential sensitivity to LLM/internet image quality, and reliance on specific models are minor drawbacks that could be addressed in future work. On the other hand, the modular design and clear improvements over baselines are significant. Given this assessment, the paper warrants a score on the higher end.

### StreamVLN: Streaming Vision-and-Language Navigation via SlowFast Context Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05240v1)
- **Authors**: Meng Wei, Chenyang Wan, Xiqian Yu, Tai Wang, Yuqiang Yang, Xiaohan Mao, Chenming Zhu, Wenzhe Cai, Hanqing Wang, Yilun Chen, Xihui Liu, Jiangmiao Pang
- **Abstract**: Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) in real-world settings requires agents to process continuous visual streams and generate actions with low latency grounded in language instructions. While Video-based Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) have driven recent progress, current VLN methods based on Video-LLM often face trade-offs among fine-grained visual understanding, long-term context modeling and computational efficiency. We introduce StreamVLN, a streaming VLN framework that employs a hybrid slow-fast context modeling strategy to support multi-modal reasoning over interleaved vision, language and action inputs. The fast-streaming dialogue context facilitates responsive action generation through a sliding-window of active dialogues, while the slow-updating memory context compresses historical visual states using a 3D-aware token pruning strategy. With this slow-fast design, StreamVLN achieves coherent multi-turn dialogue through efficient KV cache reuse, supporting long video streams with bounded context size and inference cost. Experiments on VLN-CE benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance with stable low latency, ensuring robustness and efficiency in real-world deployment. The project page is: \href{https://streamvln.github.io/}{https://streamvln.github.io/}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "StreamVLN: Streaming Vision-and-Language Navigation via SlowFast Context Modeling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces StreamVLN, a novel framework for vision-and-language navigation (VLN) designed to process continuous video streams in real-time.  StreamVLN addresses the computational challenges of using Video-LLMs for VLN by employing a "slow-fast" context modeling strategy. It uses a fast-streaming dialogue context with a sliding window KV cache for responsive action generation and a slow-updating memory context (with 3D-aware token pruning) to compress historical visual states, enabling long-term reasoning without excessive computational cost. Experiments on VLN-CE benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance while maintaining low latency, indicating the framework's potential for real-world deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the specific combination of a sliding-window dialogue context with a slow-updating, pruned memory context, optimizing for both responsiveness and long-term reasoning in a streaming VLN setting. The 3D-aware token pruning to control memory growth and allow efficient offloading and reuse of KV caches is also a significant contribution. Other VLN methods have used either sampled frames, sparse visual tokens or fixed-sized memory, without the hybrid approach.

* **Significance:** This work addresses a key challenge hindering the real-world deployment of VLN systems: efficiently processing continuous video streams with low latency. The "slow-fast" strategy presents a practical solution that balances fine-grained visual understanding, long-term context modeling, and computational efficiency. Experimental results show substantial improvements in performance and latency, which translates to a practical, real-time system. The paper includes real-world robot experiments (Unitree Go2) showcasing the system’s deployability.  The superior data efficiency compared to large-scale pre-training strategies (like HMAT) is a considerable advantage.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficient Context Management:** The slow-fast approach allows processing long video streams without unbounded context size or inference cost.
    * **Practical Real-world Evaluation:** The paper showcases the framework's deployability with real-world robot experiments, reinforcing its practicality.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The method achieves SOTA performance on the VLN-CE benchmarks while maintaining low latency.
    * **Data Efficiency:** The paper achieves high performance with less training data than other methods, suggesting a more efficient learning approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Component Complexity:** Managing the interplay of sliding window, slow updating, and token pruning adds complexity to the system, which might present difficulties for adoption and modification.
    * **Limited Generalization Scenarios:**  While the paper addresses a critical aspect of real-world deployment (real-time processing), the evaluation is still limited to a few specific scenarios. It might be more challenging to generalize across different indoor/outdoor environments. The paper notes viewpoint and occlusion as potential limitations.
    * **Dialogue Coherence Reliance on Action History:** The dependency of dialogue coherence on action history might introduce complexities in asynchronous inference and deployment.

* **Potential Influence:** The StreamVLN approach could influence future research in VLN and embodied AI by demonstrating a practical method for managing visual context in real-time. The slow-fast context modeling strategy, as well as the voxel-based token pruning technique, can be explored for other continuous interaction tasks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of VLN by addressing the critical challenge of real-time processing of continuous video streams. The slow-fast context modeling strategy is a practical and effective solution that balances competing demands on computational resources and performance. The strong empirical results, data efficiency, and real-world robot experiments support the paper's claims and demonstrate the potential of StreamVLN for real-world deployment.

However, the complexity of the system, the limited generalization scenarios, and reliance on action history represent some limitations. While the token pruning seems promising, the improvement in VLN performance is only marginal. While the method is SOTA, the improvements against recent approaches are not drastic. Therefore, while the paper makes a valuable contribution, it is not a paradigm shift that merits a higher score. The 7.5 score reflects that the paper presents a solid, practical, and well-validated approach with clear strengths, but also acknowledges the limitations and potential challenges for broader adoption and generalization.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### When Chain of Thought is Necessary, Language Models Struggle to Evade Monitors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05246v1)
- **Authors**: Scott Emmons, Erik Jenner, David K. Elson, Rif A. Saurous, Senthooran Rajamanoharan, Heng Chen, Irhum Shafkat, Rohin Shah
- **Abstract**: While chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring is an appealing AI safety defense, recent work on "unfaithfulness" has cast doubt on its reliability. These findings highlight an important failure mode, particularly when CoT acts as a post-hoc rationalization in applications like auditing for bias. However, for the distinct problem of runtime monitoring to prevent severe harm, we argue the key property is not faithfulness but monitorability. To this end, we introduce a conceptual framework distinguishing CoT-as-rationalization from CoT-as-computation. We expect that certain classes of severe harm will require complex, multi-step reasoning that necessitates CoT-as-computation. Replicating the experimental setups of prior work, we increase the difficulty of the bad behavior to enforce this necessity condition; this forces the model to expose its reasoning, making it monitorable. We then present methodology guidelines to stress-test CoT monitoring against deliberate evasion. Applying these guidelines, we find that models can learn to obscure their intentions, but only when given significant help, such as detailed human-written strategies or iterative optimization against the monitor. We conclude that, while not infallible, CoT monitoring offers a substantial layer of defense that requires active protection and continued stress-testing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the reliability of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) monitoring as an AI safety defense mechanism.  While CoT monitoring, where the reasoning process of AI models is examined to prevent harm, is intuitively appealing, recent studies have questioned its faithfulness. This paper argues that *monitorability* is the key property, not just faithfulness, for CoT monitoring to be effective in preventing severe harm at runtime. They introduce a conceptual framework that distinguishes between "CoT-as-rationalization" (post-hoc explanation) and "CoT-as-computation" (integral to the reasoning process). They hypothesize that complex tasks requiring CoT-as-computation will be more monitorable.  The authors conduct experiments, replicating previous work but increasing task difficulty to necessitate CoT, and find that this resolves unfaithfulness issues, as the model is forced to expose its reasoning for difficult tasks. They also develop methodology guidelines to stress-test CoT monitoring against deliberate evasion and find that models can learn to obscure intentions, but only with significant external help (human written strategies or iterative optimization). The conclusion is that CoT monitoring, while not foolproof, is a substantial defense that requires continued stress-testing and active protection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its reframing of the CoT monitoring problem.  The shift from focusing on faithfulness to monitorability is a valuable contribution.  The distinction between CoT-as-rationalization and CoT-as-computation provides a useful lens for understanding the limitations of CoT monitoring and identifying when it's most likely to be effective. The stress-testing methodology guidelines are a good starting point for further research.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is in its attempt to address criticisms of CoT monitoring and offer a more nuanced perspective.  By demonstrating that CoT can be a reliable defense *under specific conditions* (complex tasks, continued stress-testing), the paper provides a more realistic assessment of its potential. The guidelines for stress-testing monitors are important and potentially very useful for future safety research. The paper directly addresses the growing concerns of AI safety community, making this a timely and relevant contribution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Conceptual Framework:** The distinction between CoT-as-rationalization and CoT-as-computation is well-defined and helpful.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments, though limited in scope, support the claim that increasing task complexity enhances monitorability.
    *   **Methodological Contribution:** The stress-testing methodology guidelines are valuable for researchers developing and evaluating CoT monitors.
    *   **Realistic Assessment:** The paper avoids overly optimistic claims and acknowledges the limitations of CoT monitoring, making it a more credible contribution to the safety literature.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Task Scope:** The experimental environments (GPQA, stealth email, math) are somewhat artificial and might not fully represent the complexity of real-world scenarios. While complex for current LLMs, these are toy domains.
    *   **Reliance on Current LLM Capabilities:** The argument that CoT-as-computation is necessary relies on the limitations of current transformer architectures. Future advancements may render this distinction less relevant.
    *   **Human Help Reliance:** The experiments showed that deliberate evasion requires significant external help, which might make it harder to study with current methods.
    *   **Oversight-Pattern Simplistic:** Oversimplification of the "oversight_pattern" environment reduces complexity to a minimum for CoT to be needed, which makes the evaluation simplistic.
    *    **Evaluation is not exhaustive**: More methods to evaluate are needed such as what happens when models have access to black-box tools for example code generation, as CoT is unlikely to be able to monitor code.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely influence future research on CoT monitoring and AI safety by encouraging a more nuanced approach that considers task complexity and monitorability. The stress-testing methodology could become a standard practice for evaluating CoT monitors.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of AI safety by reframing the debate on CoT monitoring and providing a practical methodology for stress-testing monitors. While the empirical evidence is limited and the reliance on current LLM capabilities is a potential weakness, the conceptual framework and methodological contribution justify a relatively high score. It doesn't completely revolutionize the field, but it offers a valuable and timely re-assessment.

### Response Attack: Exploiting Contextual Priming to Jailbreak Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05248v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqi Miao, Lijun Li, Yuan Xiong, Zhenhua Liu, Pengyu Zhu, Jing Shao
- **Abstract**: Contextual priming, where earlier stimuli covertly bias later judgments, offers an unexplored attack surface for large language models (LLMs). We uncover a contextual priming vulnerability in which the previous response in the dialogue can steer its subsequent behavior toward policy-violating content. Building on this insight, we propose Response Attack, which uses an auxiliary LLM to generate a mildly harmful response to a paraphrased version of the original malicious query. They are then formatted into the dialogue and followed by a succinct trigger prompt, thereby priming the target model to generate harmful content. Across eight open-source and proprietary LLMs, RA consistently outperforms seven state-of-the-art jailbreak techniques, achieving higher attack success rates. To mitigate this threat, we construct and release a context-aware safety fine-tuning dataset, which significantly reduces the attack success rate while preserving model capabilities. The code and data are available at https://github.com/Dtc7w3PQ/Response-Attack.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Response Attack: Exploiting Contextual Priming to Jailbreak Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new jailbreak attack called "Response Attack" (RA) that leverages contextual priming in large language models (LLMs). RA involves crafting a dialogue where an initial, mildly harmful response (generated by an auxiliary LLM) primes the target LLM to subsequently generate more harmful content in response to a trigger prompt. The authors demonstrate that RA outperforms existing jailbreak techniques across various open-source and proprietary LLMs. To mitigate this vulnerability, they create a context-aware safety fine-tuning dataset that reduces the attack success rate while preserving model capabilities. The paper makes claims about novel vulnerability discovery and its mitigations through safety fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of exploiting contextual priming for jailbreaking LLMs is genuinely novel. While in-context manipulation and dialogue-based attacks have been explored, this paper specifically highlights the impact of preceding responses, drawing an analogy to psychological priming. Formalizing this vulnerability and presenting the RA framework represents a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** Demonstrating that contextual priming can lead to significant vulnerabilities in safety-aligned LLMs has clear significance. The high attack success rates achieved by RA suggest current alignment techniques are inadequate in handling this specific type of adversarial context. The creation of a context-aware safety fine-tuning dataset as a potential mitigation strategy is also valuable, especially if its utility and generalizability hold up in real-world applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of contextual priming vulnerabilities.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering a wide range of LLMs and comparing against established baselines. RA's consistent outperformance is compelling.
    *   **Practical Mitigation:** The context-aware safety fine-tuning dataset offers a concrete step toward addressing the vulnerability.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The release of code and data promotes reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Auxiliary LLM:** RA requires an auxiliary model to generate the priming responses. The choice of this model could significantly impact the attack's effectiveness, potentially limiting its real-world applicability if the auxiliary model requirements are highly specific.
    *   **Prompt Template Engineering:** RA relies on handcrafted prompt templates for generating the initial prompts and trigger prompts. While the authors provide examples, these templates might not generalize across all tasks or harmful intents, requiring manual adaptation and refinement which adds a burden of "human intuition and expertise" that they state limits its adaptability.
    *   **Limited Mitigation Evaluation:** While the safety fine-tuning dataset reduces the attack success rate, the paper does not rigorously assess the potential side effects or trade-offs associated with this approach. Potential issues include unintended behavioral changes, reduced helpfulness, or the creation of new vulnerabilities.
    *   **Generalizability of Mitigation:** The evaluation of safety fine-tuning is limited to one specific LLM (Llama-3-8B-Instruct). Its effectiveness on other models remains unclear.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely influence future research on LLM safety and alignment. It highlights a gap in current defenses and motivates the development of context-aware safety mechanisms. The safety fine-tuning dataset could serve as a valuable resource for the research community.

**Justification:**

RA makes a useful contribution, but its somewhat limited scope and reliance on handcrafted elements prevent it from being a truly transformative advancement. The necessity of templates is a limitation that requires more human involvement, which contradicts their aim for scalability. The mitigation strategy, while a good first step, lacks thorough analysis.

### Open Vision Reasoner: Transferring Linguistic Cognitive Behavior for Visual Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05255v1)
- **Authors**: Yana Wei, Liang Zhao, Jianjian Sun, Kangheng Lin, Jisheng Yin, Jingcheng Hu, Yinmin Zhang, En Yu, Haoran Lv, Zejia Weng, Jia Wang, Chunrui Han, Yuang Peng, Qi Han, Zheng Ge, Xiangyu Zhang, Daxin Jiang, Vishal M. Patel
- **Abstract**: The remarkable reasoning capability of large language models (LLMs) stems from cognitive behaviors that emerge through reinforcement with verifiable rewards. This work investigates how to transfer this principle to Multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) to unlock advanced visual reasoning. We introduce a two-stage paradigm built on Qwen2.5-VL-7B: a massive linguistic cold-start fine-tuning, followed by multimodal reinforcement learning (RL) spanning nearly 1,000 steps, surpassing all previous open-source efforts in scale. This pioneering work reveals three fundamental insights: 1) Behavior transfer emerges surprisingly early in cold start due to linguistic mental imagery. 2) Cold start broadly memorizes visual behaviors, while RL critically discerns and scales up effective patterns. 3) Transfer strategically favors high-utility behaviors such as visual reflection. Our resulting model, Open-Vision-Reasoner (OVR), achieves state-of-the-art performance on a suite of reasoning benchmarks, including 95.3% on MATH500, 51.8% on MathVision and 54.6% on MathVerse. We release our model, data, and training dynamics to catalyze the development of more capable, behavior-aligned multimodal reasoners.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Open Vision Reasoner (OVR), a Multimodal LLM (MLLM) built on Qwen2.5-VL-7B, designed to enhance visual reasoning by transferring cognitive behaviors from language models. The approach involves a two-stage training process: a linguistic cold-start fine-tuning followed by multimodal reinforcement learning (RL).  The authors find that behavior transfer emerges early in the cold start, driven by linguistic mental imagery; that cold start memorizes behaviors, while RL discerns and scales them, and that transfer favors high-utility behaviors like visual reflection. OVR achieves state-of-the-art performance on reasoning benchmarks like MATH500, MathVision, and MathVerse, and the authors release the model and training data to encourage further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of transferring cognitive behaviors from language models to MLLMs via a two-stage training process has a reasonable level of novelty.  The findings about the early emergence of behavior transfer due to linguistic mental imagery and the differential roles of cold start (memorization) and RL (discrimination/scaling) add further nuances. The approach is related to prior "RL with cold start" paradigms, but it is applied with unprecedented scale and a detailed behavior transfer analysis.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is derived from several factors.  First, demonstrating state-of-the-art results on challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks highlights the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The release of the model, data, and training dynamics is a significant contribution, as it enables others to build upon and validate these findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The paper employs a systematic two-stage training pipeline with a carefully curated dataset. The scale of the multimodal RL is noteworthy.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:**  The in-depth analysis of visual cognitive behaviors provides valuable insights into the transfer and evolution of reasoning skills within MLLMs.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The model achieves competitive performance on several benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Open Source Contribution:** Releasing the model, training data, and experimental details is a significant contribution to the research community.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results are impressive, the paper builds upon existing frameworks like "RL with cold start" and open-source LLMs. The gains, while meaningful, may not represent a paradigm shift. It can be argued that the core conceptual innovation is more modest than radical.
    *   **Limited Insight on Underlying Mechanisms:**  While the paper identifies the roles of different stages and the transfer of high-utility behaviors, a deeper understanding of *how* specific linguistic structures trigger visual cognitive behaviors within the MLLM would strengthen the work.
    *   **Dependence on a Specific LLM:** Although they chose a strong open-source model (Qwen2.5-VL-7B), the transferability of the methodology to other base MLLMs needs further investigation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of future research in visual reasoning and MLLMs. The insights into cognitive behavior transfer and the open-source resources will likely stimulate further exploration in this area. The demonstration of scaling RL for visual reasoning with impressive results could also encourage others to adopt similar strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a valuable contribution to the field, demonstrating strong empirical results, an insightful analysis of cognitive behavior transfer, and a commitment to open-source research. While the overall methodology builds on existing techniques, the scale, rigorous analysis, and performance gains warrant a high score. It is, however, not a complete paradigm shift; rather it presents a clear and effective way to approach visual reasoning with MLLMs.

### Spatio-Temporal LLM: Reasoning about Environments and Actions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05258v1)
- **Authors**: Haozhen Zheng, Beitong Tian, Mingyuan Wu, Zhenggang Tang, Klara Nahrstedt, Alex Schwing
- **Abstract**: Despite the significant recent progress of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), MLLMs still struggle to correctly answer prompts that require a holistic spatio-temporal understanding. Specifically, it is challenging to address prompts that refer to 1) the entirety of an environment that an agent equipped with an MLLM can operate in; and simultaneously also refer to 2) recent actions that just happened and are encoded in a video clip. However, such a holistic spatio-temporal understanding is important for agents operating in the real world. To address this issue, we first develop a framework to collect a large-scale dataset. Using the collected "Reasoning about Environments and Actions" (REA) dataset, we show that recent methods indeed struggle to correctly answer the prompts. To improve, we develop a "spatio-temporal LLM" (ST-LLM), a model equipped with projectors to improve both spatial understanding of an environment and temporal understanding of recent observations. On the collected REA data, we show that the proposed method significantly improves results compared to prior work. Code and data are available at https://zoezheng126.github.io/STLLM-website/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Spatio-Temporal LLM: Reasoning about Environments and Actions":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of equipping Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) with the ability to reason holistically about both spatial environments and temporal dynamics.  It argues that current MLLMs often struggle with prompts that require understanding the entirety of an agent's environment and reasoning about recent actions encoded in video clips. To tackle this, the authors:

1.  Introduce a new dataset called "Reasoning about Environments and Actions" (REA), collected using a semi-automatic pipeline and building on existing datasets like EPIC-Kitchens. REA focuses on spatio-temporal question answering in egocentric videos and corresponding 3D scene representations.
2.  Propose a "spatio-temporal LLM" (ST-LLM) architecture that enhances spatial and temporal understanding by incorporating 3D positional embeddings and a cross-modal alignment module. This architecture aims to fuse egocentric video features with 3D scene representations.
3.  Demonstrate through experiments that existing MLLMs struggle on REA, and that their proposed ST-LLM achieves significant performance improvements compared to baseline methods on the collected REA dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the combination of several elements:
    *   The creation of the REA dataset, which specifically targets spatio-temporal reasoning within an embodied agent's environment. While existing datasets address spatial or temporal reasoning separately, REA combines them in a way that is relevant to real-world tasks.  The data collection pipeline leverages existing datasets and introduces a good methodology for generating QA pairs.
    *   The ST-LLM architecture, which directly incorporates 3D information via point clouds into a video-language model. The use of positional encodings on both image features and point cloud ray directions is a sensible design choice, along with a cross-modal alignment module.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant limitation of current MLLMs: their inability to fully integrate spatial and temporal cues for reasoning about environments and actions. The REA dataset provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating progress in this area, and the ST-LLM architecture offers a promising approach for improving MLLM performance on these tasks. The ability to reason about actions in context is crucial for embodied AI, robotics, and other applications. However, the improvements shown, although significant, are not yet at a level that suggests a complete solution, which leaves room for future research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies a significant gap in current MLLM capabilities.
*   **REA Dataset:** The dataset is a valuable contribution, providing a targeted benchmark for spatio-temporal reasoning. The data generation process is fairly convincing, and the statistics provided support the utility of the collected data.
*   **ST-LLM Architecture:** The proposed architecture is well-motivated and combines existing techniques in a novel way.  The design choices are sensible and justified.
*   **Experimental Evaluation:** The experiments demonstrate the limitations of existing methods and the effectiveness of the proposed approach. Ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different model components.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Modest Performance Improvements:** While the ST-LLM shows improvements over baselines, the overall accuracy on the REA dataset remains relatively low. This suggests that there is still significant room for improvement in spatio-temporal reasoning capabilities. The improvement does not necessarily present a paradigm shift.
*   **Limited Architecture Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel, the individual components of the ST-LLM architecture are not entirely new. It builds on existing video-language models and incorporates relatively standard 3D processing techniques.
*   **Scalability Concerns:**  The reliance on point cloud representations for the entire environment may limit the scalability of the approach to larger and more complex environments. The data collected is specific to a kitchen environment and may not generalize well to other domains.
*   **Dataset Bias:** The dataset may contain biases due to the templated QA generation process.  While this process introduces variety, it may also constrain the diversity and naturalness of the questions and answers.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper is likely to influence future research on spatio-temporal reasoning in MLLMs. The REA dataset will serve as a valuable benchmark for evaluating new methods, and the ST-LLM architecture provides a starting point for further exploration of 3D-aware video-language models. It could lead to improvements in embodied AI, robotics, and other applications that require reasoning about actions in context.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the factors above, I assign this paper a score of **7**.

*   The problem it tackles is of significant importance to the field of MLLMs and their application in embodied AI. The collected REA dataset constitutes a valuable asset to the research community.
*   The architectural contribution, while not revolutionary, is a solid and well-motivated integration of existing techniques to solve a clearly defined problem.
*   The experimental results show improvements, albeit incremental, over existing methods.
*   The limitations mentioned above temper the impact, specifically the scalability concerns and the dataset bias.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Simple Edits: X-Planner for Complex Instruction-Based Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05259v1)
- **Authors**: Chun-Hsiao Yeh, Yilin Wang, Nanxuan Zhao, Richard Zhang, Yuheng Li, Yi Ma, Krishna Kumar Singh
- **Abstract**: Recent diffusion-based image editing methods have significantly advanced text-guided tasks but often struggle to interpret complex, indirect instructions. Moreover, current models frequently suffer from poor identity preservation, unintended edits, or rely heavily on manual masks. To address these challenges, we introduce X-Planner, a Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM)-based planning system that effectively bridges user intent with editing model capabilities. X-Planner employs chain-of-thought reasoning to systematically decompose complex instructions into simpler, clear sub-instructions. For each sub-instruction, X-Planner automatically generates precise edit types and segmentation masks, eliminating manual intervention and ensuring localized, identity-preserving edits. Additionally, we propose a novel automated pipeline for generating large-scale data to train X-Planner which achieves state-of-the-art results on both existing benchmarks and our newly introduced complex editing benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Beyond Simple Edits: X-Planner for Complex Instruction-Based Image Editing":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces X-Planner, a novel Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM)-based system designed to enhance instruction-based image editing, particularly when dealing with complex, indirect instructions.  X-Planner uses chain-of-thought reasoning to decompose complex instructions into simpler sub-instructions. For each sub-instruction, the system automatically generates edit types and segmentation masks, eliminating the need for manual intervention. A key component is the automated generation of a large-scale dataset (COMPIE) tailored for training X-Planner. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art results on existing benchmarks and on the authors' newly introduced complex editing benchmark.  The system bridges the gap between user intent and the capabilities of existing editing models and preserves original object identities and reduces over-editing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the complete system for image editing with complex instructions.
    *   **Instruction Decomposition:** The decomposition aspect itself is not entirely new (GenArtist explored this), but X-Planner's integration with image content understanding and its generation of edit types and segmentation masks is a significant advancement. This makes the whole system self-contained without relying on external detectors or segmentors. The way the MLLM reasons is still not completely apparent.

    *   **Automated Dataset Generation:** The automated pipeline for creating the COMPIE dataset is also a substantial contribution, enabling the training of the X-Planner model. Many MLLM papers now rely on high-quality datasets to achieve good performance. It also includes annotations for masks and bounding boxes for edits.
    *   **Edit Type Prediction:** The method uses pre-trained MLLMs and is therefore based on existing techniques. The added prediction of edit types makes better mask localization possible, but edit type accuracy on a large dataset would also increase the performance in this type of system.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation in existing image editing methods. Current systems often struggle with complex and indirect instructions, requiring substantial manual effort. X-Planner offers a way to automate this process and make image editing more accessible and efficient. It also enables more localized edits, preserving content that should not be modified. The release of the COMPIE dataset will likely stimulate further research in this area. The results clearly demonstrate the benefits of the combined method against current approaches.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **End-to-End System:** X-Planner is a complete system that handles complex instructions from start to finish, including decomposition, mask generation, and integration with existing editing models.
    *   **Automation:** The system minimizes the need for manual input, making image editing more efficient and user-friendly.
    *   **Performance:** X-Planner achieves state-of-the-art results on existing and new benchmarks.
    *   **Dataset:** The creation of COMPIE fills a void in the community to train these types of systems.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** X-Planner is not an image editing system. It builds on existing text-to-image diffusion models, therefore, its performance is dependent on the quality and capabilities of the underlying editing models.

    *   **MLLM Dependence:** Like most current systems, the performance relies on the MLLM's capabilities. Some examples presented in the paper are still unrealistic (a statue with golden skin and hair).
    *   **Complexity and Generalizability:** Training and deploying large MLLMs can be challenging and computationally expensive. It would be important to assess how the system adapts to scenarios outside the training distribution of COMPIE and to analyze cases where the instruction decomposition fails.
    *   **Scalability of annotation process:** While the paper creates a dataset using an annotation pipeline, GPT-4o is still used in parts of the process. Replacing GPT-4o would increase the robustness of the annotations and allow scaling the data annotations pipeline further.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of instruction-based image editing. X-Planner's approach of instruction decomposition and automated mask generation could become a standard practice. The COMPIE dataset is a valuable resource for training and evaluating future image editing systems. The system's automation and accessibility could open up new possibilities for creative expression and image manipulation.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:**

X-Planner presents a meaningful advancement in complex instruction-based image editing. The automated data generation pipeline and full system are a significant contribution. While the underlying diffusion-based editing models and MLLMs are not novel by themselves, the way the system combines these is a clear improvement over other current systems. It is well-motivated and demonstrated with strong results. The limitations surrounding MLLM performance prevent it from being higher and fully realizing its power. More analysis in failure cases for specific MLLM-generated edge cases would strengthen the paper even further.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
